## PART I — CURRENT OPERATING WEEK

<a id="hc-page-001"></a>
### CHAPTER I.01 · PAGE 001 — PERSONALIZED TIMING OVERLAY — SOURCES CHECKED

#### Evidence-checked cardio and strength timing

- **Selections:** cardio—Recommend the timing from my schedule, training load, heat, and recovery; strength—Recommend strength-workout timing from evidence and my schedule.
- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 4 unique A/B human source(s); fit indirect timing evidence.
- **Morning-versus-evening evidence:** Evening exercise showed a significantly larger reduction in blood pressure (BP) than morning exercise in hypertensive women on ARBs (evening vs morning, -4 ± 5 vs. -1 ± 5 mmHg), but no significant difference was found in those on ACEi. This suggests indirect evidence for evening timing in BP-lowering, but not generalizable to non-hypertensive or non-ARBS populations. [A | 03_sleep_stress/sleep_best_practices | 10.7759/cureus.107068]
- **Cardio timing:** Concurrent strength and endurance training may result in interference effects, particularly when performed in close sequence. The sequence of strength and endurance training (e.g., strength followed by endurance) may influence adaptations, with evidence suggesting that strength-first may preserve strength gains better than endurance-first in concurrent training. [A | 02_training_desk/concurrent_hybrid_lift_run | 10.3389/fspor.2025.1692399]
- **Strength timing:** The sequence of strength and endurance training within a session may affect outcomes, with strength-first potentially minimizing interference. This supports prioritizing strength before endurance in a single session to preserve neuromuscular adaptations, though no direct evidence on timing for non-athletes is provided. [A | 02_training_desk/concurrent_hybrid_lift_run | 10.3389/fspor.2025.1692399]
- **Concurrent-training separation:** The "interference effect" refers to suboptimal gains in strength or endurance when both are trained concurrently. Factors such as training volume, intensity, and sequence (e.g., strength before endurance) may modulate this effect, but no evidence supports a timing advantage for strength when not combined with endurance. [A | 02_training_desk/concurrent_hybrid_lift_run | 10.3389/fspor.2025.1692399]
- **Sleep and Dallas-heat tradeoffs:** Heat adaptation regimes improve performance in the heat, reduce core and skin temperature, and lower heart rate. These adaptations are measurable via HR monitoring and may benefit performance, but no direct evidence links timing of training to heat adaptation in non-athletes. [A | 02_training_desk/heat_acclimatization | 10.1007/s40279-023-01831-2]
- **Recommendation and why:** PLANNING DEFAULT: Use the existing 17:00 window for full cardio and strength sessions due to schedule feasibility. This does not imply superiority of evening timing; evidence from S1 on BP reduction in ARBs users is indirect and not applicable to non-hypertensive, non-ARBS populations. No timing evidence supports morning over evening for strength or cardio in this profile. [A | 02_training_desk/concurrent_hybrid_lift_run | 10.3389/fspor.2025.1692399] [A | 03_sleep_stress/sleep_best_practices | 10.7759/cureus.107068]


This overlay controls **when** cardio and strength are placed; the base week below controls **what** is performed and preserves its interference/skip rules. A morning or split selection becomes executable only when the recorded clock details resolve the 04:50–05:50 Anki and 06:00 work conflict; otherwise the existing 17:00 window remains the planning default.

<a id="hc-page-002"></a>
### CHAPTER I.02 · PAGE 002 — WEEK OPERATING PLAN

This date-agnostic operating week is for a Black male, age 26, in Dallas, cutting from about 230 lb to 200 lb in Phase A, then 190 lb in Phase B, using three progressive lifting days, running as primary cardio, and biking for easy volume or substitution. Start with today’s card—even Thursday—and continue forward; missed sessions never cancel daily light, protein, creatine, hydration, steps, easy movement, or the sleep window and are never stacked onto the next day.

<a id="hc-page-003"></a>
### CHAPTER I.03 · PAGE 003 — LOCKED NUMBERS

- Wake: **04:30 every day**; outdoor light is **04:35–04:50**.
- Bed: wind-down begins **19:15**; lights out is **20:15 every day**.
- Time in bed: **8.25 hours**; no weekend sleep-in or sleep-restriction block.
- Work: **Monday–Thursday, 06:00–16:00**; Friday–Sunday are off.
- Training: default start **17:00** and default duration **75 minutes**, unless the day card specifies a shorter session.
- Active cut: **Phase A, about 230 lb → 200 lb**. Phase B is 200 lb → 190 lb only after Phase A is stable for 2–3 weeks and lifts are not crashing.
- Protein: Phase A goal **200 lb = 90.7 kg**; **90.7 kg × 1.8 g/kg = 163 g**, rounded to **165 g/day**. Use four timed feedings of 40–45 g, with breakfast at least 40 g. Phase A floor is 145 g and heavy-lower/high-mileage cap is 200 g; this week’s printed target remains 165 g.
- Creatine: **5 g every day**, no loading; immediately post-session on training days and with dinner on a full rest-from-loading day.
- Caffeine: optional; last dose is **11:15**. No evening caffeinated pre-workout.
- Steps: default floor **10,000 every day**; Saturday follows the long-run exception and Sunday is **8,000–10,000 easy steps**.
- Heart rate: Tanaka only—**208 − (0.7 × 26) = 189.8**, rounded to **HRmax 190**. Zone 2 is **114–133 bpm**.
- Zone 2 governors: full-sentence talk test first, sustainable nasal breathing second, and 114–133 bpm as a check. Talk test wins when they disagree; the same rules govern running and biking.
- Hydration: **3–4 L/day baseline**, with additional fluid for sweat and more sodium from food in Dallas heat.

<a id="hc-page-005"></a>
### CHAPTER I.05 · PAGE 005 — DAILY CARDS

#### Monday

##### Clock

- **04:30:** Wake and begin hydration.
- **04:35–04:50:** Outdoor light while walking easily; the walk counts toward steps.
- **04:50–05:50:** Study/Anki.
- **05:50:** Breakfast and first protein feeding.
- **06:00–16:00:** Work. Walk for 5 minutes near 08:00, 10:00, 13:00, and 15:00.
- **11:00:** Mid-work protein meal; any optional caffeine ends by 11:15.
- **11:20–11:40:** Lunch walk outside when heat is safe, otherwise indoors.
- **16:10:** Pre-lift protein feeding plus familiar carbohydrate.
- **16:20–16:40:** Easy transition walk.
- **17:00–18:15:** Lift A.
- **18:15:** Immediate post-lift protein feeding plus creatine 5 g; dinner follows.
- **18:40–19:05:** Easy step-filling walk only if needed.
- **19:15:** Wind-down.
- **20:15:** Lights out.

##### Session

- Back squat: **4×5 at 3–5 RIR**.
- Romanian deadlift: **4×8 at 2–3 RIR**.
- Leg press: **3×12 at 2 RIR**.
- Leg curl: **3×12–15 at 1–2 RIR**.
- Rest **2–3 minutes** for squat/RDL and **60–90 seconds** for accessories. Progress by adding a clean rep or small load only while prescribed RIR remains. No run or conditioning finisher.

##### Bike

- **None.** Lower-body lifting is the leg load today. Do not add an easy spin, hard ride, or bike finisher.

##### Steps / NEAT

- Floor: **10,000 easy steps**.
- Use the 15-minute light walk, four 5-minute work walks, 20-minute lunch walk, 20-minute transition walk, and 10–25 minutes after lifting.
- Check the counter after lifting and finish the shortfall before 19:15. Never turn Monday walking into tempo work.

##### Fuel

- Protein: **165 g total** from four timed feedings—45 g at 05:50, 40 g at 11:00, 40 g pre-lift, and 40 g immediately post-lift/dinner. Eat by schedule, not tirzepatide-suppressed hunger.
- Put most discretionary carbohydrate around the pre/post-lift feedings; include vegetables and fiber at the main meals.
- Keep a measured-TDEE deficit modest. Protein and training fuel come before optional snack calories.
- Drink **3–4 L across the day**, adding fluid for sweat and more sodium through food in heat.

##### If I skip today’s session

- Still complete 165 g protein, creatine 5 g with dinner, morning light, 10,000 steps, hydration, and 20:15 bed.
- Do not move Lift A to Tuesday and do not add a run/lift double.
- Add at most 20 minutes of the missed lower pattern to Wednesday only if recovered; otherwise the session is gone.
- No 21:00 makeup. Sleep wins.

##### Done when

- [ ] Woke at 04:30 and completed outdoor light at 04:35.
- [ ] Completed 165 g protein, 3–4 L baseline fluid, and creatine 5 g.
- [ ] Completed Lift A or used the skip rule without doubling.
- [ ] Reached 10,000 easy steps.
- [ ] Started wind-down at 19:15 and lights out at 20:15.

#### Tuesday

##### Clock

- **04:30:** Wake and begin hydration.
- **04:35–04:50:** Outdoor light and easy walking.
- **04:50–05:50:** Study/Anki.
- **05:50:** Breakfast and first protein feeding.
- **06:00–16:00:** Work, with 5-minute walks near 08:00, 10:00, 13:00, and 15:00.
- **11:00:** Mid-work protein meal; optional caffeine ends by 11:15.
- **11:20–11:40:** Lunch walk outdoors when safe or indoors in heat.
- **16:10:** Pre-session protein feeding with familiar carbohydrate.
- **16:20–16:40:** Easy walk and change for the selected mode.
- **17:00–17:45/18:15:** Zone 2 run for 45–75 minutes, or indoor Zone 2 bike for 60–75 minutes.
- **Immediately after:** Post-session protein feeding and creatine 5 g; dinner follows.
- **19:15:** Wind-down.
- **20:15:** Lights out.

##### Session

- Preferred mode: **Zone 2 run for 45–75 minutes**, RPE about 3–4/10.
- Full sentences must remain possible; nasal breathing should be sustainable. Use 114–133 bpm as a check.
- Begin and end with 5 minutes very easy. No tempo finish, intervals, or heavy lifting.
- If heat or leg soreness makes running the wrong choice, the bike substitution below is the complete session.

##### Bike

- **This is the session only when substituting for the run:** indoor bike **60–75 minutes Zone 2**, using the same talk test and 114–133 bpm check.
- Keep cadence comfortable and resistance low enough for full sentences. Do not run and ride on the same day to “earn more.”

##### Steps / NEAT

- Floor: **10,000**, with run steps counted. Bike minutes do not erase the walking floor.
- Keep the light walk, four work walks, and 20-minute lunch walk.
- After a run, fill only a small shortfall with easy walking. After a bike, plan 20–30 extra easy walking minutes before 19:15.

##### Fuel

- Protein: **165 g total** from four timed feedings—45 g breakfast, 40 g at 11:00, 40 g pre-session, and 40 g immediately post-session/dinner. Appetite is not the trigger.
- Bias carbohydrate around the selected run or ride. Include vegetables/fiber at the 11:00 meal and dinner.
- Maintain a modest measured-TDEE deficit without stripping the fuel needed for 45–75 easy aerobic minutes.
- Drink **3–4 L baseline**, plus sweat replacement and more sodium from food in Dallas heat.

##### If I skip today’s session

- Morning light, 165 g protein, creatine 5 g with dinner, hydration, 10,000 steps, and sleep still execute.
- Do not make Thursday a long run or add two Thursday modes.
- If Thursday becomes easy rather than quality, add at most 10–15 easy minutes; otherwise Tuesday is gone.
- A hot/sore-day easy bike counts as Tuesday completed, not skipped.

##### Done when

- [ ] Woke at 04:30 and completed the light block.
- [ ] Completed 165 g protein, baseline fluid, and creatine 5 g.
- [ ] Completed either the Zone 2 run or the Zone 2 bike—not both—or followed the skip rule.
- [ ] Reached 10,000 steps.
- [ ] Kept 19:15 wind-down and 20:15 lights out.

#### Wednesday

##### Clock

- **04:30:** Wake and begin hydration.
- **04:35–04:50:** Outdoor light plus easy walking.
- **04:50–05:50:** Study/Anki.
- **05:50:** Breakfast and first protein feeding.
- **06:00–16:00:** Work, with 5-minute walks near 08:00, 10:00, 13:00, and 15:00.
- **11:00:** Mid-work protein meal; optional caffeine ends by 11:15.
- **11:20–11:40:** Lunch walk.
- **16:10:** Pre-lift protein feeding plus carbohydrate.
- **16:25–16:40:** Optional 10–15 minute very easy spin; otherwise walk easily.
- **17:00–18:15:** Lift B.
- **18:15:** Immediate post-lift protein feeding plus creatine 5 g; dinner follows.
- **18:40–19:05:** Easy step-filling walk only if needed.
- **19:15:** Wind-down.
- **20:15:** Lights out.

##### Session

- Row: **4×6**.
- Pull-up: **3×6**.
- Bench press: **4×6**.
- Overhead press: **3×8**.
- Face pull: **3×15**.
- Optional curl plus extension: **3×10–12 each**, only if all work ends by 18:15.
- Keep compounds at **2–3 RIR**. Rest 2–3 minutes on compounds and 60–90 seconds on accessories. No hard cardio.

##### Bike

- **Optional easy only:** 10–15 minutes at Zone 1/low Zone 2 before the lift, with full conversation.
- Skip it if legs remain heavy from Tuesday or if it threatens the 17:00 lift, immediate post-lift feeding, or 19:15 wind-down.

##### Steps / NEAT

- Floor: **10,000 easy steps**.
- Use morning light, four work walks, the 20-minute lunch walk, the pre-lift walk/spin transition, and a 10–25 minute post-lift walk.
- A spin contributes aerobic minutes but not step count; verify 10,000 before wind-down.

##### Fuel

- Protein: **165 g total** from four timed feedings—45 g at breakfast, 40 g at 11:00, 40 g pre-lift, and 40 g immediately post-lift/dinner.
- Place carbohydrate around lifting; include vegetables/fiber at the main meals. Eat protein on the timer despite low appetite.
- Keep the cut modest enough that row and bench performance do not collapse.
- Drink **3–4 L baseline**, plus additional fluid for sweat and more sodium from food in heat.

##### If I skip today’s session

- Still execute morning light, 165 g protein, creatine 5 g with dinner, 10,000 steps, hydration, and the sleep window.
- Do not move the full upper session to Thursday.
- Add at most 20 minutes of missed push/pull work to Friday if recovered; Friday must remain light for Saturday.
- No late-night makeup.

##### Done when

- [ ] Woke at 04:30 and completed outdoor light.
- [ ] Completed 165 g protein, baseline hydration, and creatine 5 g.
- [ ] Completed Lift B or followed the skip rule; any bike stayed easy and optional.
- [ ] Reached 10,000 steps.
- [ ] Began wind-down at 19:15 and lights out at 20:15.

#### Thursday

##### Clock

- **04:30:** Wake and begin hydration.
- **04:35–04:50:** Outdoor light and easy walking.
- **04:50–05:50:** Study/Anki.
- **05:50:** Breakfast and first protein feeding.
- **06:00–16:00:** Work, with 5-minute walks near 08:00, 10:00, 13:00, and 15:00.
- **11:00:** Mid-work protein meal; optional caffeine ends by 11:15.
- **11:20–11:40:** Lunch walk indoors or outside when heat is safe.
- **16:10:** Pre-quality protein feeding and familiar carbohydrate.
- **16:20–16:40:** Easy transition walk.
- **17:00–17:45/17:50:** One quality mode: run intervals or indoor bike intervals. If crushed, use the shorter backup or 45 minutes easy bike.
- **Immediately after:** Post-session protein feeding and creatine 5 g; dinner follows.
- **19:15:** Wind-down.
- **20:15:** Lights out.

##### Session

- Pick **one quality template** and repeat that choice for 4–8 weeks when conditions allow.
- **Run VO2:** warm up 10–15 minutes easy; complete **5–6 × 3 minutes hard / 2–3 minutes easy jog**; cool down 10 minutes. Hard means only 2–3 word phrases, not conversation. Total about 45–50 minutes.
- **Bike VO2:** use the same interval/recovery/warmup/cooldown structure on an indoor bike at roughly **85–95 rpm**; hard breathing matches the run intervals.
- **Backup when crushed:** **10–12 × 1 minute brisk / 1 minute easy**, or convert the entire day to **45 minutes easy Zone 2 bike**. No long run, heavy legs, or second quality session.

##### Bike

- **This is the session** when bike intervals replace run intervals because of heat, shin/knee irritation, or accumulated run fatigue.
- Do not perform bike intervals after run intervals. One quality mode means one warmup, one work set, and one cooldown.

##### Steps / NEAT

- Floor: **10,000 whether quality happens or not**.
- Use the light walk, four work walks, 20-minute lunch walk, and 20-minute after-work walk.
- If the session is skipped or changed to a 20-minute easy spin, add indoor/easy walking and complete the step floor before 19:15.
- Thursday is not a rest-from-living day.

##### Fuel

- Protein: **165 g total** from four timed feedings—45 g breakfast, 40 g at 11:00, 40 g before quality work, and 40 g immediately post-session/dinner.
- Bias carbohydrate around the selected quality mode. On the easy backup, keep protein unchanged and use a normal carb portion.
- Poor energy availability is corrected with more food and/or less intensity, never another supplement.
- Drink **3–4 L baseline**, add sweat fluid, and use more sodium from food in heat.

##### If I skip today’s session

- Still complete 165 g protein, creatine 5 g with dinner, morning light, hydration, 10,000 steps, and 20:15 bed.
- Do not move intervals to Friday or Saturday. Friday remains Lift C; Saturday remains an easy long run.
- If only 20 minutes exist, perform 20 minutes easy jogging or spinning and finish the step floor. That is done-enough.
- If energy availability is poor, walk or spin easily, eat every protein feeding, and go to bed on time. That is the correct choice.

##### Done when

- [ ] Woke at 04:30 and completed the 04:35 light block.
- [ ] Completed 165 g protein, baseline hydration, and creatine 5 g.
- [ ] Completed exactly one quality mode, one backup, or the explicit low-energy skip.
- [ ] Reached 10,000 steps even if quality was skipped.
- [ ] Kept 19:15 wind-down and 20:15 lights out.

#### Friday

##### Clock

- **04:30:** Wake and begin hydration; no weekend sleep-in.
- **04:35–04:50:** Outdoor light and easy walking.
- **04:50–05:50:** Study/Anki.
- **05:50:** Breakfast and first protein feeding.
- **06:10–07:00:** Work-free block: 30-minute easy walk, then light household reset.
- **07:00–10:30:** Personal tasks/errands, changing position or walking briefly each hour.
- **10:30–10:45:** Easy walk.
- **11:00:** Second protein feeding; optional caffeine ends by 11:15.
- **11:20–15:00:** Recovery/free block with one 20-minute easy walk.
- **15:15–15:45:** Default easy walk; optional flat easy bike may replace this block only under the Bike rule below.
- **16:10:** Pre-lift protein feeding plus carbohydrate.
- **17:00–18:00/18:15:** Lift C.
- **Immediately after:** Post-lift protein feeding plus creatine 5 g; dinner follows.
- **19:15:** Wind-down.
- **20:15:** Lights out.

##### Session

- Easy trap-bar deadlift: **3×8 at about 4 RIR**, explicitly lighter than Monday and never ground to failure.
- Push pattern: **3×8 at 3 RIR**.
- Chest-supported row: **3×10 at 3 RIR**.
- Lateral raises: **3×15 at 2–3 RIR**.
- Rest about 2 minutes for trap-bar/push/row and 60–90 seconds for laterals. End able to run Saturday; no heavy squat/hinge, intervals, or conditioning finisher.

##### Bike

- **Default: none. Optional easy only:** 20–30 minutes flat Zone 2 at conversational effort, ending by 15:45.
- Use it only when legs feel normal and Saturday is not a long-run progression/PR attempt. It never follows the lift and is never hard.

##### Steps / NEAT

- Floor: **10,000 easy steps**.
- Use the light walk, 30-minute morning walk, 15-minute pre-meal walk, 20-minute free-block walk, and 30-minute afternoon walk.
- If the optional bike replaces the afternoon walk, bike time does not count as steps; add easy household/indoor walking before 19:15.

##### Fuel

- Protein: **165 g total** from four timed feedings—45 g breakfast, 40 g at 11:00, 40 g pre-lift, and 40 g immediately post-lift/dinner.
- Bias carbohydrate toward the lift and Saturday preparation. Keep vegetables/fiber in the two main meals.
- Do not deepen the measured-TDEE deficit before the long run. Scheduled food outranks low appetite.
- Drink **3–4 L baseline**, plus sweat fluid and more sodium from food in heat.

##### If I skip today’s session

- Keep morning light, 165 g protein, creatine 5 g with dinner, hydration, 10,000 steps, and 20:15 bed.
- Do not put Lift C before or after Saturday’s long run.
- Add at most 20 minutes of missed push/pull work to Monday if recovered; otherwise Friday is gone.
- Do not replace the missed lift with hard biking or intervals.

##### Done when

- [ ] Woke at 04:30 and completed morning light without sleeping in.
- [ ] Completed 165 g protein, baseline fluid, and creatine 5 g.
- [ ] Completed lighter Lift C or used its skip rule; any bike stayed optional and easy.
- [ ] Reached 10,000 easy steps.
- [ ] Preserved 19:15 wind-down and 20:15 lights out.

#### Saturday

##### Clock

- **04:30:** Wake and begin hydration; no weekend sleep-in.
- **04:35–04:50:** Outdoor light plus easy walking.
- **04:50–05:50:** Study/Anki.
- **05:50:** Breakfast and first protein feeding.
- **06:10–11:00:** Work-free block: prepare familiar run clothing, route, fluids, and food; complete only light errands.
- **11:00:** Second protein feeding; optional caffeine ends by 11:15.
- **11:30–15:45:** Recovery/free block with brief easy movement rather than sitting the whole afternoon.
- **16:00:** Pre-run protein feeding plus familiar carbohydrate.
- **17:00:** Long run; duration is current long-run duration plus no more than 10 minutes.
- **Immediately after:** Post-run protein feeding and creatine 5 g; dinner follows.
- **19:15:** Wind-down. Add the optional 10 minutes only when the run still permits immediate feeding, dinner, and this wind-down.
- **20:15:** Lights out.

##### Session

- Run the current established long-run duration plus **at most 10 minutes** versus last week.
- Keep almost all of it Zone 2, RPE about 3–4/10, with full-sentence conversation governing effort.
- The last 20 minutes may become only slightly quicker if Thursday did not cook the legs, Friday remained light, and normal form is intact.
- If Thursday quality or Friday lower work left heavy legs, keep Saturday entirely easy and shorter. No lift, intervals, hard bike, or second workout.

##### Bike

- **None.** Do not ride hard after the long run and do not add an easy ride as a second endurance session.

##### Steps / NEAT

- The run and normal walking should produce about **8,000–10,000 steps**.
- If the run is short, fill to 10,000 with easy walking. If it is long enough to reach the range, do not force another march or chase 15,000.
- Keep brief easy movement during the free block; do not sit the entire day before or after the run.

##### Fuel

- Protein: **165 g total** from four timed feedings—45 g breakfast, 40 g at 11:00, 40 g pre-run, and 40 g immediately post-run/dinner.
- Bias carbohydrate toward breakfast, the 11:00 meal, pre-run feeding, and dinner. For a run longer than 90 minutes, use only familiar, previously tolerated run fuel and fluid.
- Do not accelerate the cut by underfueling the primary long run. Keep high-fiber food away from the immediate pre-run window.
- Drink **3–4 L baseline across the day**, then add run-specific fluid and more sodium from food for sweat/heat.

##### If I skip today’s session

- Still complete morning light, 165 g protein, creatine 5 g with dinner, hydration, easy movement, and the sleep window.
- Do not move the long run to Sunday.
- Sunday remains mobility, meal prep, 8,000–10,000 steps, and only optional easy biking.
- A later-week replacement may be 40–50 minutes easy, never a panic long run.

##### Done when

- [ ] Woke at 04:30 and completed light plus Anki.
- [ ] Completed 165 g protein, baseline hydration, and creatine 5 g.
- [ ] Completed the controlled long run or applied the no-Sunday-makeup rule.
- [ ] Followed the long-run step rule without chasing 15,000 or adding a bike.
- [ ] Began wind-down at 19:15 and lights out at 20:15.

#### Sunday

##### Clock

- **04:30:** Wake and begin hydration; no weekend sleep-in.
- **04:35–04:50:** Outdoor light and easy walking.
- **04:50–05:13:** Mobility swap using the sequence below.
- **05:13–05:50:** Quiet weekly review/Anki cleanup.
- **05:50:** Breakfast and first protein feeding.
- **06:10–06:30:** Easy walk.
- **08:30–10:30:** Meal prep, including four daily protein feedings for workdays.
- **11:00:** Second protein feeding; optional caffeine ends by 11:15.
- **11:20–15:30:** Work-free recovery block with a 20–30 minute easy walk near 13:00.
- **15:30:** Third protein feeding.
- **17:00–17:30/17:45:** Easy walk, or optional easy Zone 2 bike for 30–45 minutes.
- **Immediately after optional ride, or 18:00 without it:** Final protein feeding, dinner, and creatine 5 g.
- **19:15:** Wind-down and prepare Monday.
- **20:15:** Lights out.

##### Session

- Rest from loading: **no lifting and no intervals**.
- Hips, 10 minutes: easy 90/90 transitions, couch stretch, and glute bridges.
- T-spine/shoulders, 8 minutes: open books, wall slides, and unloaded face-pull motion.
- Walk-out, 5 minutes: easy walking after mobility.
- Complete meal prep. Do not make up Thursday quality or Saturday’s long run.

##### Bike

- **Optional easy only:** indoor or flat bike for **30–45 minutes Zone 2**, using full sentences and 114–133 bpm only as a check.
- This is the preferred extra biking slot because impact is low. It is not intervals, a spin class, or a replacement for the 8,000–10,000 walking target.

##### Steps / NEAT

- Target **8,000–10,000 easy steps**; rest from loading never means zero movement.
- Use morning light, the 5-minute mobility walk-out, 20-minute post-breakfast walk, 20–30 minute midday walk, and ordinary meal-prep/household movement.
- If choosing the bike, complete the same walking target. Fill any shortfall before 19:15 without turning it into cardio.

##### Fuel

- Protein: **165 g total** from four timed feedings—45 g at breakfast, 40 g at 11:00, 40 g at 15:30, and 40 g with dinner. Use timers rather than hunger.
- Take creatine 5 g after the optional ride with dinner, or with dinner on a bike-free rest day.
- Include carbohydrate to recover from Saturday and support Monday lower-body work; include vegetables/fiber in meal-prep portions.
- Drink **3–4 L baseline**, adding fluid for heat. Do not use Sunday restriction to compensate for one high-calorie meal.

##### If I skip today’s session

- Still complete outdoor light, 165 g protein, creatine 5 g with dinner, meal prep, hydration, 8,000–10,000 steps, and normal sleep.
- Do not replace mobility or the optional easy bike with a missed long run, lift, or VO2 session.
- If mobility is missed, complete one easy 5-minute round before 19:15; do not turn it into training.
- Prepare Monday as written rather than punishing the missed work.

##### Done when

- [ ] Woke at 04:30 and completed outdoor light without sleeping in.
- [ ] Completed mobility/rest-from-loading and Sunday meal prep.
- [ ] Completed 165 g protein, baseline fluid, and creatine 5 g.
- [ ] Reached 8,000–10,000 easy steps; any bike stayed optional Zone 2.
- [ ] Started wind-down at 19:15 and lights out at 20:15.

<a id="hc-page-006"></a>
### CHAPTER I.06 · PAGE 006 — DESK-DAY MOVEMENT (Mon–Thu)

- Change between sitting and standing every **30–60 minutes** when work permits; standing still is a posture change, not a step substitute.
- Put **5-minute walking breaks** on the calendar near 08:00, 10:00, 13:00, and 15:00.
- After the 11:00 protein meal, walk **15–20 minutes** around 11:20. Use indoor corridors when Dallas heat makes outdoor walking unsafe.
- Count the 04:35 light walk, lunch walk, work breaks, 16:20 transition walk, and 10-minute post-training cooldown walk.
- Check steps at 16:00 and after training. Fill a shortfall with easy indoor/outdoor walking before 19:15; never schedule a 10,000-step block at 21:00.

<a id="hc-page-007"></a>
### CHAPTER I.07 · PAGE 007 — HEAT + BIKE SUBSTITUTION CARD

- Morning outdoor light is a short circadian cue, not unprotected midday baking. Keep it at 04:35 and use shade/clothing as needed.
- Tuesday heat or sore legs: replace the easy run with **60–75 minutes indoor Zone 2 bike**. That bike is Tuesday’s full session, not an addition.
- Thursday ugly heat index, shin/knee irritation, or accumulated run fatigue: replace run VO2 with the single indoor bike VO2 template. If recovery is poor, shrink to the 1-minute backup or 45 minutes easy.
- Saturday remains the primary long-run day. Prefer a treadmill or a shorter easy run when outdoor heat is unsafe; if running is not safe, an easy indoor bike may substitute, but it never becomes hard or creates a second quality day.
- Sunday bike is **30–45 minutes easy Zone 2 only**. Friday bike is default-off and, when used, easy/flat. Never ride hard Friday, Saturday after the run, or Sunday.
- Keep the same 04:30 wake. Heat never deletes steps—indoor walking counts—and never authorizes a salt-tablet stack.

<a id="hc-page-008"></a>
### CHAPTER I.08 · PAGE 008 — RED-S AND PHASE RULES

- Remain in **Phase A** until the weekly average is near 200 lb, that level has been stable for **2–3 weeks**, and squat/row/bench performance is not crashing. One 200 lb weigh-in does not start Phase B.
- Phase A uses **165 g protein daily**, with a 145 g absolute poor-appetite floor and up to 200 g only on a justified heavy-lower/high-mileage day. Do not cut protein to accelerate loss.
- Phase B targets 190 lb and uses **155 g protein daily**, with a 138 g floor and 190 g high-mileage/heavy-lower cap. Change cards to 155 only when Phase B actually begins.
- Keep average loss around **0.5–1.0% bodyweight per week**. If average weight falls faster than about 2 lb/week for two weeks, add **150–200 kcal/day** rather than adding cardio.
- Preserve three progressive lift days and roughly **10–16 hard weekly sets per major muscle group** through compound overlap. Visible tone is fat removed while muscle and bar speed remain.
- If two or more persist longer than seven days—rising morning HR, collapsing session quality, lost libido, cold/dizziness, broken sleep, or falling strength while weight falls over 1%/week—drop Thursday quality to easy bike/walk, shorten Saturday, and eat more.
- Underfueling is never fixed with a fourth lift, a “fat-burn” spin class, evening stimulants, or lower protein.

<a id="hc-page-009"></a>
### CHAPTER I.09 · PAGE 009 — WHAT THIS WEEK IS NOT

- Not a six-day bodybuilding split and not a fourth lifting day.
- Not a second interval day or a hard-run plus hard-bike stack.
- Not a peptide cycle.
- Not sleep restriction or a weekend sleep-in; the clock remains 20:15–04:30.
- Not a 16:8 schedule that deletes the 05:50 protein breakfast.
- Not “Black-specific” exotic sets, reps, VO2 genetics, or added supplements.
- Not Friday heavy legs, a squat PR, or hard biking before Saturday’s long run.
- Not punishment: a skipped session never cancels light, protein, creatine, hydration, steps, easy movement, or sleep.

<a id="hc-page-010"></a>
### CHAPTER I.10 · PAGE 010 — OPTIONAL FOOTNOTE

- Coverage weak: no retrieved passages were needed to compile this locked execution plan.
- Protein, progressive lifting, sleep consistency, morning light, Zone 2 volume, and one weekly quality session are the only foundations used.
- Because this user is a Black indoor worker, a clinician-measured 25(OH)D level may be worth discussing; Dallas sun does not prove sufficiency.
- Routine blood-pressure screening is also worth discussing, especially when heat or stimulant/vasodilator exposure is relevant.
- No personal lab value is assumed, and no additional supplement is required by this week.

<a id="hc-page-012"></a>
### CHAPTER II.02 · PAGE 012 — 1. LIBRARY FINDINGS FOR THIS CUT

- **Settled enough to operate:** during athlete weight loss, the retrieved expert literature converges on a gradual deficit, progressive resistance training, protein at 1.6–2.4 g/kg/day, and distribution across multiple high-quality-protein feedings. [A \| 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands \| 10.1007/s40279-025-02285-4] [A \| 12_population_AA \| 10.1002/jcsm.70167]
- **Settled enough to protect the plan:** two controlled male studies found that low energy availability can impair performance and produce fatigue or endocrine changes; adding expenditure while food intake collapses is therefore not a neutral edit. [B \| 02_training_desk/energy_availability_reds \| 10.1080/15502783.2022.2065111] [B \| 02_training_desk/energy_availability_reds \| 10.1139/apnm-2024-0037]
- **Tirzepatide changes execution:** a SURMOUNT post-hoc analysis reports large modeled reductions in energy intake and flags nutritional adequacy, while a tirzepatide review reports nausea, diarrhea, and constipation as common adverse events. [B \| 12_population_AA \| 10.1016/j.obpill.2026.100248] [A \| 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins \| 10.1503/cmaj.250502]
- **Coffee/caffeine is optional, not foundational:** meta-analyses support small acute exercise-performance benefits, but one controlled morning study did not show uniform cognitive-battery improvement and late high doses worsened sleep in a small male crossover study. [A \| 04_cognition_learning/caffeine \| 10.3389/fphys.2026.1858094] [A \| 04_cognition_learning/caffeine \| 10.3389/fnut.2025.1745472] [B \| 02_training_desk/concurrent_hybrid_lift_run \| 10.3390/nu18060954] [C \| 04_cognition_learning/caffeine \| 10.3389/fnut.2025.1659220]
- **Pasteurized dairy is a vehicle, not a special fat-loss agent:** retrieved milk-type evidence concerns mortality or bone outcomes and does not establish skim, whole, or ultrafiltered milk as uniquely superior for his cut. [A \| 01_food_inflammation/milk_types_comparison \| 10.3389/fnut.2026.1731841] [B \| 01_food_inflammation/milk_types_comparison \| 10.3389/fnut.2026.1785177]
- **Raw milk has the wrong evidence asymmetry:** retrieved passages show unpasteurized-dairy association with clinical brucellosis and pathogenic organisms in raw-milk samples, while no retrieved passage demonstrates a unique body-composition advantage. [C \| 01_food_inflammation/raw_milk_safety \| 10.36519/idcm.2026.984] [C \| 01_food_inflammation/raw_milk_safety \| 10.3389/fmicb.2026.1748367]
- **Not settled:** the library does not establish a personal TDEE, fiber gram target, ideal coffee dose, cut-specific milk type, goat-milk advantage, cheat-day effect size, or blind vitamin-D/magnesium/omega-3 dose; the retrieved studies on those questions are absent, indirect, or bundled. [A \| 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands \| 10.1007/s40279-025-02285-4] [B \| 02_training_desk/concurrent_hybrid_lift_run \| 10.1007/s00394-026-03983-z] [C \| 01_food_inflammation/goat_milk \| 10.1186/s12937-025-01183-9]

<a id="hc-page-013"></a>
### CHAPTER II.03 · PAGE 013 — 2. TARGETS

- **Protein — EVIDENCE AMOUNT:** **1.6–2.4 g/kg/day** during weight loss, commonly spread across **4–6 feeds** from high-quality sources. [A \| 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands \| 10.1007/s40279-025-02285-4]
- **Protein — PLANNING AMOUNT, Phase A:** 200 lb = 90.7 kg; `90.7 × 1.6 = 145 g/day` and `90.7 × 2.4 = 218 g/day`. Use `90.7 × 1.8 = 163 g`, rounded to **165 g/day**, as the repeatable planning point inside the evidence range. This is not a claim that 1.8 g/kg is uniquely optimal.
- **Meal protein — EVIDENCE AMOUNT:** retrieved guidance commonly reports **20–40 g** or **0.4–0.5 g/kg per meal** and spreading protein through the day. [A \| 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands \| 10.1007/s40279-025-02285-4] **PLANNING AMOUNT:** four label-checked feedings near **40–45 g**.
- **Phase sequence — PLANNING DEFAULT:** run Phase A from approximately **230 to 200 lb**; treat **190 lb** as a later Phase B destination, not the mass used in this generation's arithmetic.
- **Loss rate — EVIDENCE AMOUNT:** athlete papers commonly recommend roughly **0.5–1.0 kg (1–2 lb) per week**, while an obesity consensus reports a similar initial rate with a **500–750 kcal/day** reduction. [A \| 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands \| 10.1007/s40279-025-02285-4] [A \| 12_population_AA \| 10.1002/jcsm.70167] **PLANNING DEFAULT:** use a seven-day scale average and do not fabricate a TDEE.
- **Carbohydrate — EVIDENCE AMOUNT:** the athlete review gives a broad periodized range of **3–12 g/kg/day** and reports **3–6 g/kg/day** in several body-fat-reduction papers. [A \| 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands \| 10.1007/s40279-025-02285-4] **PLANNING AMOUNT:** use ordinary starch or fruit around demanding training; do not turn the broad evidence range into a fixed 272–544 g mandate without measured workload and intake.
- **Fiber:** high-fiber, low-energy-density foods are supported, but the retrieved passages do not establish his personal gram target. [A \| 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands \| 10.1007/s40279-025-02285-4] **PLANNING AMOUNT:** vegetables or fruit at three eating events plus one measured whole-grain serving.
- **Caffeine — EVIDENCE AMOUNTS:** exercise studies commonly tested about **3–6 mg/kg**, a morning male study used **300 mg (2.8–4.3 mg/kg)** about one hour before testing, and the late-evening rowing study tested **3, 6, and 9 mg/kg**. [A \| 04_cognition_learning/caffeine \| 10.3389/fphys.2026.1858094] [B \| 02_training_desk/concurrent_hybrid_lift_run \| 10.3390/nu18060954] [C \| 04_cognition_learning/caffeine \| 10.3389/fnut.2025.1659220] These are study amounts, not a personal coffee prescription.
- **Caffeine cutoff — LOCKED PLANNING DEFAULT:** optional coffee only in the morning and none after **11:15**. Moderate and high evening doses impaired subjective sleep and raised next-day sleepiness in a small trained-male study. [C \| 04_cognition_learning/caffeine \| 10.3389/fnut.2025.1659220]
- **Energy-availability guardrail:** controlled male data show worsening power/endurance, well-being, fatigue, or testosterone signals under acute low energy availability. [B \| 02_training_desk/energy_availability_reds \| 10.1080/15502783.2022.2065111] [B \| 02_training_desk/energy_availability_reds \| 10.1139/apnm-2024-0037] **PLANNING DEFAULT:** if food intake and training quality collapse together, reduce optional cardio before adding another supplement.

<a id="hc-page-014"></a>
### CHAPTER II.04 · PAGE 014 — 3. COFFEE MODULE (REQUIRED)

#### What to drink

- **PLANNING DEFAULT:** Costco whole-bean or ground coffee prepared as black drip or iced coffee. “Costco” and the exact SKU are shopping logistics, not physiology.
- **PLANNING DEFAULT:** an unsweetened bottled cold brew is acceptable when its label fits the morning caffeine plan; product caffeine varies, so the package number—not cup color or size—controls.
- **PLANNING DEFAULT:** add a small splash of **pasteurized** milk if desired. Tea may replace coffee, but it still counts toward the same 11:15 caffeine cutoff.
- Coffee is a caffeine vehicle; the retrieved performance papers studied caffeine in capsules or beverages and do not prove black coffee is a superior delivery form. [A \| 04_cognition_learning/caffeine \| 10.3389/fphys.2026.1858094]

#### What is not a staple

- Sugar-sweetened coffee drinks are not a default: retrieved dietary-pattern guidance reduces sugar-sweetened drinks, and a dental guideline recommends avoiding drinks with added sugar because intake tracks with caries. [A \| 01_food_inflammation/meal_prep_deficit_athletic \| 10.3389/fnut.2026.1795350] [A \| 06_hair_teeth/teeth_remineralization_caries \| 10.1007/s00784-026-06880-1]
- **Butter/MCT coffee:** COVERAGE GAP — no on-topic passage established a fat-loss advantage, so it is not added as a staple.
- **Raw-milk coffee:** **DO NOT USE**; adding raw milk to hot or iced coffee does not erase the unpasteurized-dairy safety signal in the retrieved evidence. [C \| 01_food_inflammation/raw_milk_safety \| 10.36519/idcm.2026.984] [C \| 01_food_inflammation/raw_milk_safety \| 10.3389/fmicb.2026.1748367]

#### Timing against the clock

- Acute caffeine improves high-intensity interval performance and cycling performance on average, but effect sizes are small and the evidence does not require daily use. [A \| 04_cognition_learning/caffeine \| 10.3389/fphys.2026.1858094] [A \| 04_cognition_learning/caffeine \| 10.3389/fnut.2025.1745472]
- A 14-man morning study using 300 mg improved several strength/power measures but did not show uniform superiority across the cognitive battery; this is not evidence that coffee is required for Anki. [B \| 02_training_desk/concurrent_hybrid_lift_run \| 10.3390/nu18060954]
- Moderate and high caffeine doses taken for a 19:00–20:00 rowing trial impaired subjective sleep and raised next-day sleepiness; evening caffeine conflicts with the 20:15 bed. [C \| 04_cognition_learning/caffeine \| 10.3389/fnut.2025.1659220]
- **PLANNING DEFAULT:** one coffee **with or immediately after the 05:50 breakfast**; a second small serving is optional only if the product label and personal tolerance fit. Nothing caffeinated after **11:15**, and no 17:00 pre-workout caffeine.

#### Tirzepatide, fasting, and teeth

- Tirzepatide commonly causes nausea, diarrhea, and constipation, but no retrieved passage tested fasted coffee specifically during tirzepatide treatment. [A \| 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins \| 10.1503/cmaj.250502]
- **PLANNING / GLP-1 GI:** attach coffee to the 05:50 breakfast if fasted coffee feels nauseating; stop the coffee rather than deleting the protein meal. This is symptom-based logistics, not a proven interaction treatment.
- The usable dental evidence concerns added sugar rather than black coffee itself; frequent sugary foods/drinks and added-sugar drinks are the relevant avoidable exposure. [A \| 06_hair_teeth/teeth_remineralization_caries \| 10.1007/s00784-026-06880-1]

<a id="hc-page-015"></a>
### CHAPTER II.05 · PAGE 015 — 4. MILK MODULE (REQUIRED) — PASTEURIZED VS RAW VS GOAT VS ULTRAFILTERED

#### Pasteurized cow's milk and fermented dairy

- Retrieved milk-type evidence does not establish a cut-specific winner: a large prospective-cohort meta-analysis addressed mortality, while a 40-woman pilot comparing full-fat milk with yogurt found no significant between-group bone-density difference. [A \| 01_food_inflammation/milk_types_comparison \| 10.3389/fnut.2026.1731841] [B \| 01_food_inflammation/milk_types_comparison \| 10.3389/fnut.2026.1785177]
- Yogurt, cheese, milk, eggs, fish, and lean meat appear inside retrieved dietary patterns, but those pattern studies do not prove any one item is indispensable. [A \| 01_food_inflammation/meal_prep_deficit_athletic \| 10.3389/fnut.2026.1795350] [A \| 12_population_AA \| 10.1002/jcsm.70167]
- **PLANNING AMOUNT:** choose pasteurized skim/low-fat or ultrafiltered milk by its label and use **8 fl oz** as a countable protein vehicle; use **1 cup yogurt** or **1 cup cottage cheese** as alternatives. “Fairlife-style” means a label-comparable pasteurized ultrafiltered product, not an evidence-backed brand endorsement.
- **PLANNING DEFAULT:** the lower-calorie label is easier to fit when two products provide similar protein. This is arithmetic and shopping logistics, not evidence that skim milk causes greater fat loss.

#### Raw milk verdict

- A human brucellosis series found the disease strongly associated with consumption of unpasteurized dairy products; unpasteurized fresh cheese was reported by 79.4% of patients in that endemic-region series. [C \| 01_food_inflammation/raw_milk_safety \| 10.36519/idcm.2026.984]
- A separate milk-sampling study detected *E. coli* in 28.82% of milk/milk-product samples, with higher prevalence in raw milk at 31.15%; some isolates carried virulence genes, showed cytotoxic effects, or were multidrug resistant. [C \| 01_food_inflammation/raw_milk_safety \| 10.3389/fmicb.2026.1748367]
- The retrieved library provides no human evidence that raw milk improves fat loss, muscle retention, running, cognition, or tirzepatide tolerance more than pasteurized milk.
- **VERDICT: DO NOT DRINK RAW MILK.** The retrieved evidence shows avoidable pathogen exposure and no demonstrated body-composition advantage. [C \| 01_food_inflammation/raw_milk_safety \| 10.36519/idcm.2026.984] [C \| 01_food_inflammation/raw_milk_safety \| 10.3389/fmicb.2026.1748367]
- **DO NOT USE** raw farm milk, raw colostrum, or unpasteurized cheese as a cut tool. [C \| 01_food_inflammation/raw_milk_safety \| 10.36519/idcm.2026.984]

#### Goat milk

- The only superficially relevant human goat-milk hit was a protocol for an infant-formula trial without results; it cannot support an adult intolerance, body-composition, or performance claim. [C \| 01_food_inflammation/goat_milk \| 10.1186/s12937-025-01183-9]
- **VERDICT: COVERAGE GAP — no goat-milk recommendation from this library.** If chosen as a preference, it must be pasteurized and is only a **PLANNING** milk substitute; no superiority is claimed.

#### Fermented dairy, vegetables, and colostrum

- A healthy-adult crossover trial used 100 g/day of fresh or pasteurized sauerkraut for four weeks and found the overall microbiome relatively resilient, with changes in individual species and increased serum short-chain fatty acids only after pasteurized sauerkraut. [B \| 01_food_inflammation/fermented_veg_sauerkraut_kimchi \| 10.1186/s40168-024-02016-3]
- The small yogurt-versus-milk pilot did not establish a between-group bone-density advantage. [B \| 01_food_inflammation/milk_types_comparison \| 10.3389/fnut.2026.1785177]
- **PLANNING DEFAULT:** plain pasteurized yogurt and pasteurized sauerkraut may be ordinary foods if tolerated; neither is required and neither gets a fat-loss claim.
- Bovine-colostrum evidence concerns exercise-associated gut or immune markers, including a 16-man acute prolonged-cycling trial, not superior body composition or routine recovery. [A \| 07_supplements/bovine_colostrum \| 10.1371/journal.pone.0266379] [B \| 07_supplements/bovine_colostrum \| 10.1007/s00394-026-04016-5]
- **COLOSTRUM VERDICT: SKIP.** The relevant studies do not establish a benefit for his primary cut goals, and raw colostrum is not recommended. [A \| 07_supplements/bovine_colostrum \| 10.1371/journal.pone.0266379] [B \| 07_supplements/bovine_colostrum \| 10.1007/s00394-026-04016-5]

<a id="hc-page-017"></a>
### CHAPTER II.07 · PAGE 017 — 6. DEFAULT DAY (LOW PLANNING)

**PLANNING DEFAULT — Mon–Thu clock; all quantities and protein totals are label-checked planning estimates.**

- **05:50 breakfast + coffee:** 3 eggs, 1 cup plain Greek yogurt, berries, and 1/2 cup dry oats; one black drip/iced coffee or coffee with a measured splash of pasteurized milk. **PLANNING AMOUNT:** approximately **40 g protein**. Coffee is optional; no breakfast deletion.
- **11:00 desk lunch:** 6 oz cooked poultry, 1 cup cooked rice or one potato, and 1–2 cups frozen vegetables. **PLANNING AMOUNT:** approximately **47 g protein**; running total **87 g**.
- **16:10 pre-training feeding:** one whey serving blended with 8 fl oz pasteurized ultrafiltered milk plus a banana. **PLANNING AMOUNT:** approximately **38 g protein**; running total **125 g**. If dairy volume is poorly tolerated, use water and move the missing label protein into dinner.
- **Immediately post-training / dinner:** 5–6 oz fish, lean beef, or poultry with a measured starch and vegetables. **PLANNING AMOUNT:** approximately **40 g protein**; day total **165 g**.
- **Creatine:** **LOCKED PLANNING AMOUNT:** 5 g with the post-training feeding; with dinner on a non-training day.
- **Caffeine:** **LOCKED PLANNING DEFAULT:** first coffee at 05:50; any second serving ends by 11:15. No caffeine for the 17:00 lift/run.

**Why this shape:** Four protein feedings operationalize retrieved guidance to spread protein across four to six daily feeds. [A \| 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands \| 10.1007/s40279-025-02285-4] Scheduled eating responds to the documented reduction in energy intake during tirzepatide treatment; the specific clock and portions are planning, not a tirzepatide meal-timing trial. [B \| 12_population_AA \| 10.1016/j.obpill.2026.100248] Using an ordinary starch/fruit serving near training is a **PLANNING DEFAULT** based on broad carbohydrate periodization; a retrieved pre-lift meal trial did not establish that a higher-carbohydrate meal always improves performance. [A \| 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands \| 10.1007/s40279-025-02285-4] [B \| 07_supplements/supplement_timing \| 10.1002/ejsc.12274] Smaller, split portions are **PLANNING / GLP-1 GI**, because nausea, diarrhea, and constipation are documented but these exact plates were not tested. [A \| 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins \| 10.1503/cmaj.250502]

<a id="hc-page-018"></a>
### CHAPTER II.08 · PAGE 018 — 7. 45-MIN SUNDAY PREP

**PLANNING DEFAULT — one timer, Costco-first, no physiological claims:**

1. **00:00–00:05:** start rice or potatoes; preheat oven/air fryer; place seven containers and a marker on the counter.
2. **00:05–00:12:** season a poultry tray or portion plain rotisserie-chicken meat. Use the ready-cooked option when raw-food cook time would exceed the 45-minute cap.
3. **00:12–00:18:** portion frozen vegetables into four work-lunch containers.
4. **00:18–00:25:** portion oats, berries, and pasteurized yogurt for four breakfasts; leave eggs for quick cooking or use pre-cooked eggs.
5. **00:25–00:31:** place canned fish beside two backup meal containers; portion cooked beef/fish only if already available.
6. **00:31–00:37:** divide cooked poultry and starch across lunches; mark each container with its package-label protein estimate.
7. **00:37–00:41:** put coffee beans/grounds, filters, mug, and shaker in the morning station; refrigerate pasteurized milk and dairy.
8. **00:41–00:45:** stage four whey servings, freeze overflow meals, and post the daily checkpoints: **40 / 87 / 125 / 165 g — PLANNING AMOUNTS**.

<a id="hc-page-019"></a>
### CHAPTER II.09 · PAGE 019 — 8. WHOLE-FOOD TREATS

The obesity consensus reports that adherence, rather than one universally superior diet pattern, is central to long-term weight-loss maintenance. [A \| 12_population_AA \| 10.1002/jcsm.70167] The athlete review prioritizes protein and high-fiber, lower-energy-density foods during body-composition work. [A \| 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands \| 10.1007/s40279-025-02285-4] **PLANNING DEFAULT:** a treat fits only as a pre-portioned part of the planned intake after the day's protein structure is covered; the recipes themselves were not tested.

- **Oat-banana-whey cookies — PLANNING:** mix one banana, 1 cup oats, one whey serving, cocoa, and enough pasteurized milk or water to bind; bake eight cookies. Calculate the batch from labels and pre-portion two-cookie bags.
- **Date-cocoa bites — PLANNING:** combine four chopped dates, 1/2 cup oats, cocoa, and enough plain yogurt to bind; make eight bites. Dates are a chosen sweet ingredient, not an evidence-backed fat-loss food.
- **Yogurt bark — PLANNING:** spread 1 cup plain pasteurized Greek yogurt with 1/2 cup berries, freeze, then divide before eating. Use the package label for protein.
- **Coffee treat — PLANNING:** cold brew over ice with a measured pasteurized-milk splash and cocoa; no syrup by default. This remains inside the 11:15 cutoff.

<a id="hc-page-020"></a>
### CHAPTER II.10 · PAGE 020 — 9. EAT / RARELY

#### EAT

- Hit the planned protein total through four to six high-quality-protein feedings. [A \| 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands \| 10.1007/s40279-025-02285-4]
- Use poultry, eggs, fish, pasteurized dairy, lean meat, and whey as interchangeable protein vehicles; the rotation is **PLANNING**, while the protein target is evidence-rooted. [A \| 01_food_inflammation/meal_prep_deficit_athletic \| 10.3389/fnut.2026.1795350] [A \| 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands \| 10.1007/s40279-025-02285-4]
- Use vegetables, fruit, whole grains, and measured nuts/olive oil as the default food pattern. [A \| 12_population_AA \| 10.1002/jcsm.70167]
- Keep potatoes and white rice available as **PLANNING** carbohydrate vehicles; the retrieved library does not justify banning them.
- Drink black coffee or coffee with pasteurized milk only if wanted, in the morning window; caffeine is optional despite acute performance evidence. [A \| 04_cognition_learning/caffeine \| 10.3389/fphys.2026.1858094]
- Split a meal if volume is difficult. **PLANNING / GLP-1 GI.**

#### RARELY

- **Raw milk, raw colostrum, and unpasteurized cheese: DO NOT DRINK/USE.** Retrieved clinical and food-sampling evidence shows unpasteurized-dairy and raw-milk pathogen risk without a demonstrated cut advantage. [C \| 01_food_inflammation/raw_milk_safety \| 10.36519/idcm.2026.984] [C \| 01_food_inflammation/raw_milk_safety \| 10.3389/fmicb.2026.1748367]
- Sugary lattes, frappes, sweet bottled coffees, and other sugar-added drinks are not staples; retrieved dietary guidance reduces sugar-sweetened drinks, and dental guidance recommends avoiding added-sugar beverages. [A \| 01_food_inflammation/meal_prep_deficit_athletic \| 10.3389/fnut.2026.1795350] [A \| 06_hair_teeth/teeth_remineralization_caries \| 10.1007/s00784-026-06880-1]
- Frequent sweets, refined foods, fried foods, and processed meats were reduced in retrieved Mediterranean-style interventions. [A \| 01_food_inflammation/meal_prep_deficit_athletic \| 10.3389/fnut.2026.1795350]
- High-UPF exposure is associated with adverse metabolic outcomes in retrieved observational synthesis, but the library does not establish a personal UPF gram cutoff. [A \| 01_food_inflammation/foods_to_exclude_upf \| 10.4093/dmj.2025.0925]
- **Alcohol — COVERAGE GAP:** the named alcohol folder did not return usable human evidence for recovery, sleep timing, or a safe performance dose. **PLANNING DEFAULT:** this document treats alcohol as off and does not generate a drinking protocol.
- Very large or unusually rich meals that personally worsen nausea are **PLANNING / GLP-1 GI**, not a paper-backed universal food ban.
- Lean red meat is limited/rotated, not prohibited, in the retrieved dietary patterns. [A \| 12_population_AA \| 10.1002/jcsm.70167]

<a id="hc-page-021"></a>
### CHAPTER II.11 · PAGE 021 — 10. ONE CHEAT DAY

**COVERAGE GAP:** no usable direct human trial established the effect size of one weekly cheat day, an ideal placement after a long run, or a special “refeed” food.

The retrieved obesity consensus supports a sustained energy deficit and reports that adherence is central to long-term maintenance; it does not validate an uncontrolled weekly cheat day. [A \| 12_population_AA \| 10.1002/jcsm.70167]

**PLANNING ARITHMETIC — hypothetical, not from a trial:** six days at `−500 kcal/day` plus one day at `+1,500 kcal above maintenance` equals `6 × −500 + 1,500 = −1,500 kcal` for the week. This illustrates how one day can shrink a weekly deficit; it does not predict his fat change or authorize the surplus.

**PLANNING DEFAULT:** choose one logged restaurant meal or one pre-portioned treat, preserve the normal protein feedings, and return to the normal plan at the next meal. Saturday placement is allowed only as scheduling convenience, not a claimed metabolic advantage. Do not add punishment intervals, starve the next day, or use a raw-milk “refeed.”

A large intake reduction plus gastrointestinal symptoms can create a starvation state in GLP-1 users; this is documented in one counterfeit-semaglutide case and is not a frequency estimate for prescribed tirzepatide. [C \| 08_peptides_gray/uncertified_quality_risk \| 10.7759/cureus.102627] **PLANNING / safety boundary:** persistent vomiting or inability to keep food or fluids down is outside cheat-day planning; do not answer it with punishment exercise, and seek clinical guidance.

<a id="hc-page-022"></a>
### CHAPTER II.12 · PAGE 022 — 11. FOOD × LOCKED STACK

#### Tirzepatide

- Tirzepatide produces substantial weight loss in adults with obesity, while nausea, diarrhea, and constipation are common adverse events. [A \| 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins \| 10.1503/cmaj.250502]
- Modeled energy-intake reductions as large as roughly 1,200 kcal/day have been reported from semaglutide/tirzepatide trial data, and the SURMOUNT post-hoc analysis emphasizes balanced dietary intake while noting that micronutrients were not systematically assessed. [B \| 12_population_AA \| 10.1016/j.obpill.2026.100248]
- **Coffee:** no retrieved passage establishes a direct tirzepatide–coffee interaction. **PLANNING / GLP-1 GI:** take morning coffee with breakfast if fasted coffee feels nauseating; omit coffee before omitting food.
- **Milk:** no retrieved passage establishes that cow, goat, or ultrafiltered milk improves tirzepatide efficacy. **PLANNING / GLP-1 GI:** use a smaller pasteurized portion or a non-dairy protein vehicle if milk volume is personally difficult.
- **Stacking incretins — PLANNING / locked-stack constraint:** no liraglutide, semaglutide, retatrutide, cagrilintide, or survodutide is added on top of active tirzepatide; the current medication stays clinician-managed.

#### Creatine monohydrate

- Retrieved sources support creatine for resistance-training strength, although much of the pooled evidence is in older adults and the near-age trial was small. [A \| 07_supplements/creatine \| 10.1186/s11556-025-00384-9] [B \| 07_supplements/creatine \| 10.7759/cureus.103091]
- **LOCKED PLANNING AMOUNT:** creatine monohydrate **5 g/day**; use the post-training feeding for convenience or dinner on a rest day.
- **Coffee:** no accepted retrieved passage establishes that coffee is required, prohibited, or performance-enhancing specifically when paired with creatine; keep the 05:50 coffee and post-training creatine as separate clock events.
- **Milk:** no accepted retrieved passage establishes a milk requirement or a superior milk–creatine pairing; milk is only a counted protein vehicle.

Tadalafil and finasteride are not named in the injected profile, so no food-interaction subsection is generated and no grapefruit rule is invented.

<a id="hc-page-024"></a>
### CHAPTER II.14 · PAGE 024 — 13. WHAT NOT TO OPTIMIZE

- Do not turn the plan into a supplement hunt: the retrieved athlete review says most supplements are unnecessary relative to energy intake, carbohydrate periodization, protein, and training. [A \| 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands \| 10.1007/s40279-025-02285-4]
- Do not buy unverified pre-workout, weight-loss, or muscle-building products casually: a systematic review found roughly 9–15% of commercially available supplements tested in empirical studies contained prohibited substances or unapproved pharmacological agents, with those product categories most often implicated. [A \| 07_supplements/supplement_contamination_testing \| 10.3389/fspor.2026.1740663]
- Do not buy online or counterfeit GLP-1 products: a case report describes euglycemic starvation ketoacidosis after unsupervised counterfeit semaglutide with vomiting and reduced intake; one case establishes a hazard signal, not a frequency. [C \| 08_peptides_gray/uncertified_quality_risk \| 10.7759/cureus.102627]
- **PLANNING / locked-stack constraint:** do not stack another incretin or a GH-axis candidate on active tirzepatide; none earned an ADD decision in the retrieved evidence used here.
- Do not optimize coffee into a butter/MCT ritual, a raw-milk drink, or an evening pre-workout. Butter/MCT benefit is a coverage gap; raw milk has a pathogen signal; late caffeine conflicts with sleep evidence. [C \| 01_food_inflammation/raw_milk_safety \| 10.36519/idcm.2026.984] [C \| 04_cognition_learning/caffeine \| 10.3389/fnut.2025.1659220]
- Do not use a “detox” or antiparasitic cleanse: the permitted no-detox folder returned off-topic passages, so no evidence-based cleanse protocol can be generated. **COVERAGE GAP — not recommending from this library.**
- Do not build a semen-retention protocol for testosterone, cognition, or fat loss: the permitted folder returned off-topic passages and no usable direct evidence for those goals. **COVERAGE GAP — not recommending from this library.**
- Do not deepen the deficit to chase a single male hormone number: direct male energy-availability studies reported performance, fatigue, or endocrine deterioration under underfueling. [B \| 02_training_desk/energy_availability_reds \| 10.1080/15502783.2022.2065111] [B \| 02_training_desk/energy_availability_reds \| 10.1139/apnm-2024-0037]

<a id="hc-page-025"></a>
### CHAPTER II.15 · PAGE 025 — 14. GAPS

- **Coffee chemistry:** no accepted retrieved passage establishes a superior roast, brew method, Costco SKU, cold-brew preparation, or exact caffeine content per cup; use product labels and the locked cutoff.
- **Coffee and tirzepatide:** no direct trial tested fasted versus fed coffee, nausea, gastric emptying, or absorption during tirzepatide treatment.
- **Coffee and teeth:** retrieved dental guidance supports limiting added-sugar drinks, but it did not establish a black-coffee enamel or staining protocol.
- **Milk and the cut:** no controlled adult trial compared skim, whole, lactose-free, or ultrafiltered milk for fat loss and muscle retention with energy and protein matched.
- **Raw-milk benefit:** no usable human passage showed a body-composition, performance, cognition, or GLP-1-tolerance advantage over pasteurized milk.
- **Goat milk:** no adult result supports an intolerance, performance, or body-composition advantage; the human hit was an infant protocol.
- **Dairy tolerance:** no personal symptom log or diagnosed intolerance was supplied; the plan cannot infer whether milk, yogurt, or cottage cheese is best tolerated.
- **Protein during tirzepatide:** the athlete range is evidence-rooted, but no retrieved trial validates 165 g/day specifically in 26-year-old tirzepatide users doing concurrent training.
- **Calories:** no measured TDEE, food log, or two-week weight trend was supplied; a precise intake would be fabricated.
- **Carbohydrate:** no retrieved passage establishes his exact grams before, during, or after each 17:00 lift, interval workout, bike ride, or long run.
- **Fiber and pulses:** no personal gram target or tirzepatide-specific titration/tolerance protocol was retrieved.
- **Heat/electrolytes:** no sweat-rate, run-duration, weather-exposure, or sodium-loss data support a personal Dallas formula; the library also warns indirectly that indiscriminate overdrinking during sustained endurance can be harmful.
- **Cheat day:** no direct trial quantified the weekly fat-loss, appetite, or training effect of one planned high-calorie day.
- **Vitamin D and race:** no accepted retrieved passage establishes his 25(OH)D status or a Black-Dallas-specific dose; a measured 25(OH)D result would change the decision.
- **Blood pressure:** no personal BP data were supplied; this matters before converting caffeine or any vasoactive candidate into routine use.
- **Tadalafil and finasteride:** neither is named in the injected profile, so food-interaction and grapefruit claims were omitted.
- **Excluded candidate supplements:** vitamin C, zinc, melatonin, HMB, tongkat ali, fenugreek, and ashwagandha did not receive an ADD decision because the screened hits were off-goal, wrong-population, bundled, or insufficient to change this plate; absence from the table is not proof of safety or inefficacy.
- **Detox and semen retention:** the allowed folders were polluted by unrelated papers; no conclusion beyond coverage gap is filled from memory.

<a id="hc-page-026"></a>
### CHAPTER II.16 · PAGE 026 — 15. MEASUREMENT PROTOCOL

The usable measurement evidence is narrow. In 108 mixed-age adults, wearable and clinical bioimpedance estimates correlated with DXA in some analyses but failed important error or equivalence criteria and showed wide variability; a smart-scale or watch body-fat number is therefore not interchangeable with a laboratory reference. [C \| 15_health_maintenance/body_composition_measurement \| 10.3389/fspor.2025.1644082]

**PLANNING DEFAULT — weekly dashboard:**

- **Body weight:** weigh Monday, Wednesday, and Friday at approximately 04:30, after the bathroom and before breakfast, on the same scale and surface. Average the three readings. If a reading is missed, average the readings that exist; do not invent one.
- **Waist:** measure once Sunday morning at the navel, relaxed, before breakfast, using the same tape tension and position.
- **Strength:** record the load, completed reps, and final-set RIR for Monday squat `4×5`, Wednesday bench `4×6`, and one row work set. These are the 2–3 lift anchors; do not substitute a pump or wearable calorie estimate.
- **Aerobic anchor:** on Tuesday's easy run or bike, record duration, whether full-sentence conversation remained possible, and average HR only if the sensor produced credible data. The talk-test note remains primary.
- **Context:** note tirzepatide GI symptoms, sleep disruption, unusual heat, and any intentionally shortened session beside that week's numbers.

**PLANNING DECISION RULE:** react to a two-week pattern across the weekly weight average, waist, lift anchors, and easy-aerobic note—not to one morning's scale value. Do not let a wearable “readiness” score cancel protein, sleep, or easy walking; the retrieved library did not validate readiness-led calorie changes for this user.

<a id="hc-page-027"></a>
### CHAPTER II.17 · PAGE 027 — 16. CUT BRAKES / RED-S / TOO-FAST LOSS

Controlled male-athlete studies found that progressively reducing energy availability worsened power/performance and well-being, lowered testosterone-related measures, produced a tendency toward lower T3 in one trial, and increased fatigue in another. [B \| 02_training_desk/energy_availability_reds \| 10.1080/15502783.2022.2065111] [B \| 02_training_desk/energy_availability_reds \| 10.1139/apnm-2024-0037]

Tirzepatide trials report nausea, diarrhea, and constipation as common adverse events; one guideline also reported cholecystitis at 0.6% versus 0% with placebo in the cited obesity RCT. This identifies monitored adverse events, not his personal risk. [A \| 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins \| 10.1503/cmaj.250502] A narrative multi-agonist review estimates that lean mass can represent 25–40% of total weight lost in some GLP-1/multi-agonist trials, but that C-grade synthesis does not predict his personal body-composition split. [C \| 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins \| 10.3350/cmh.2025.0744]

**PLANNING BRAKE — apply when either condition occurs:**

- The weekly average falls faster than roughly **2 lb/week for two consecutive weeks**, which exceeds the upper edge of the 1–2 lb/week evidence amount already used in §2; or
- Two or more persist for more than seven days: falling lift performance, unusually hard easy pace, persistent fatigue, broken sleep, dizziness/cold intolerance, or reduced libido/morning erections.

**PLANNING RESPONSE:** keep the **165 g protein target and three progressive lifts**; remove Thursday quality first, shorten Saturday's long run, and add a measured **150–200 kcal/day** from ordinary food. Reassess the weekly dashboard for two weeks. Do not add a stimulant, fat burner, Lift D, or extra bike class.

Persistent or severe GI symptoms, inability to keep fluids down, or concerning abdominal pain are medication-safety questions for the prescribing clinician, not prompts to alter the tirzepatide dose inside this document. [A \| 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins \| 10.1503/cmaj.250502]

Retrospective and pharmacovigilance evidence suggests a possible association between GLP-1 therapies—including tirzepatide—and hair loss, with rapid weight loss proposed as a telogen-effluvium mechanism; causality has not been established, and reported patterns can include telogen effluvium or worsening androgenetic alopecia. [C \| 06_hair_teeth/stress_hair_loss \| 10.1007/s13555-026-01792-0] **PLANNING / safety boundary:** new diffuse shedding triggers nutrition/cut-rate review and clinician or dermatologist discussion. It does not trigger a research scalp compound, a peptide, or an automatic finasteride change.

<a id="hc-page-028"></a>
### CHAPTER II.18 · PAGE 028 — 17. PHASE A DONE / MAINTENANCE

The retrieved athlete guidance keeps gradual weight loss, adequate protein, and resistance training central to preserving performance and lean tissue. [A \| 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands \| 10.1007/s40279-025-02285-4] Tirzepatide withdrawal data also make maintenance a real phase: among 308 SURMOUNT-4 participants who had lost at least 10% before withdrawal, 82.5% regained at least 25% of the initial loss by week 88, with greater regain accompanied by reversal of cardiometabolic improvements. [B \| 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins \| 10.1001/jamainternmed.2025.6112]

**PLANNING DEFAULT at approximately 200 lb:**

- Hold the scale-average trend approximately stable for **2–3 weeks** before deciding on Phase B.
- Keep **165 g/day protein**, the same three lifting days, creatine **5 g/day**, and the existing easy-aerobic structure during the hold.
- If weight continues falling or lifts deteriorate, add **150–200 kcal/day** before considering any extra cardio.
- Start Phase B toward 190 only if the weekly average is stable, the three lift anchors are not collapsing, GI symptoms are manageable, and the Saturday session is recovering normally.
- Do not use the maintenance block to add a fourth lift, second interval day, or harder Sunday ride.

Any tirzepatide reduction or discontinuation is **CLINICIAN-ONLY**. Withdrawal evidence supports expecting a meaningful risk of weight regain, while an editorial synthesis describes returning appetite/weight-defense pressures after discontinuation; neither source authorizes a personal taper. [B \| 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins \| 10.1001/jamainternmed.2025.6112] [C \| 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins \| 10.7759/cureus.104259] Do not answer discontinuation with MK-677, another incretin, or any appetite/GH-axis secretagogue.
