<a id="hc-page-030"></a>
### CHAPTER II.20 · PAGE 030 — 19. SLEEP APNEA FLAG

The injected profile does **not** report loud snoring, witnessed breathing pauses, or unrefreshing sleep, so this document does not diagnose or presume obstructive sleep apnea.

STOP-Bang is a short screening questionnaire with high sensitivity but modest specificity in sleep-clinic and surgical populations; a positive screen is not a diagnosis. [A \| 03_sleep_stress/sleep_apnea_breathing \| 10.1371/journal.pone.0143697] In adults with obesity and diagnosed moderate-to-severe OSA, randomized tirzepatide trials reduced apnea-hypopnea index and hypoxic burden over 52 weeks; those findings do not show that medication or weight loss replaces diagnostic evaluation or established OSA care. [C \| 03_sleep_stress/sleep_apnea_breathing \| 10.1056/nejmoa2404881]

**PLANNING DEFAULT:** if the history later includes loud snoring, witnessed pauses, repeated unrefreshing sleep, or problematic daytime sleepiness, disclose it to a clinician and ask about validated screening or a sleep evaluation. **Mouth tape is not treatment:** the permitted passages supplied no evidence that mouth taping diagnoses or treats OSA, so no mouth-tape protocol is generated.

<a id="hc-page-031"></a>
### CHAPTER II.21 · PAGE 031 — 20. HEAT + ELECTROLYTES + SUBSTITUTION

Heat adaptation improved performance outcomes in a female-athlete systematic review, but sex/population fit limits direct transfer to this man. [A \| 02_training_desk/heat_acclimatization \| 10.1007/s40279-023-01831-2] An athlete-focused narrative review emphasizes substantial individual variability in heat acclimation, cooling, hydration, and fluid replacement. [C \| 02_training_desk/heat_acclimatization \| 10.3389/fphys.2025.1380645]

Sweat sodium is highly individual and can change with heat-acclimation status, sodium intake, and exercise intensity; an elite-football review therefore favors individualized assessment rather than one universal sodium prescription. [A \| 07_supplements/electrolytes_hydration \| 10.1007/s40279-026-02415-6] Exercise-associated hyponatremia can occur during or within 24 hours after prolonged activity, so indiscriminately forcing more plain water is not automatically safer. [C \| 07_supplements/electrolytes_hydration \| 10.2147/oajsm.s556848]

**PLANNING DEFAULT — Dallas execution:**

- Do not move the **04:30 wake** or delete **04:50–05:50 Anki** to manufacture a cooler run.
- If the 17:00 conditions make the Thursday run-quality session impractical, use the indoor bike with the same one-quality-session structure. It substitutes for the run; it is not added to it.
- On an unusually hot Saturday, shorten the long run or substitute an indoor Zone-2 bike. Keep the effort conversational and do not append a hard bike.
- Complete the day's step target indoors when needed; heat changes location, not the existence of easy movement.
- Use ordinary salted food and fluid access as logistics. No fixed salt-tablet dose or Dallas liters-per-hour formula is supported without sweat-rate, session-duration, and weather data.
- Do not “water load.” For a long/hot session, pre/post body-mass and actual fluid consumed may be logged for clinician or sports-dietitian review; this document does not convert one observation into a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-032"></a>
### CHAPTER II.22 · PAGE 032 — 21. INJURY, DESK, DELOAD, SICK DAYS

#### Running load

A runner-focused systematic review identifies rapid increases in weekly mileage or intensity and insufficient recovery as contributors to injury susceptibility, while also emphasizing that evidence and runner characteristics are heterogeneous. [A \| 02_training_desk/running_injury_load \| 10.3389/fspor.2026.1733815] **COVERAGE GAP:** the retrieved passage does not validate a universal “10% rule” for this user.

**PLANNING LOAD CEILING:** change one running variable at a time; Saturday remains **current long-run duration plus at most 10 minutes versus the prior week**, and a missed run is not repaid with a spike. New pain that alters gait, worsens across runs, or persists despite load reduction moves the session to an easy bike and prompts qualified assessment; this is not a self-diagnosis.

#### Desk and mobility

A workplace-intervention systematic review includes successful structures using a **5-minute break each hour** and digital prompts, while a separate review found break protocols varied widely. [A \| 02_training_desk/sedentary_software_engineer \| 10.1186/s12966-024-01595-6] [A \| 02_training_desk/sedentary_software_engineer \| 10.1007/s40279-022-01649-4]

**PLANNING DEFAULT:** once each work hour, spend five minutes on easy walking plus comfortable posture changes; use one reminder, not a new app stack. Short static stretches of roughly 30–45 seconds can improve flexibility in a sedentary sample, while longer pre-performance holds may reduce strength; this is limited evidence and not a reason to stretch aggressively before lifting. [B \| 10_recovery_fascia/daily_stretching_mobility \| 10.1038/s41598-026-36856-w]

#### Deload, NSAIDs, and illness

- **PLANNING DEFAULT DELOAD:** every **4–6 weeks**, or earlier when §16's brake pattern appears, reduce lifting work sets by approximately one-third, remove Thursday VO2, and shorten the long run by roughly one-quarter for one week. The interval and percentages are planning logistics; no accepted programming hit validated them as uniquely optimal.
- NSAID use is not a daily recovery stack. An athlete-focused review reports inconclusive ergogenic effects, possible interference with anabolic/training adaptation—particularly with high-dose ibuprofen—and gastrointestinal/cardiovascular concerns. [A \| 10_recovery_fascia/nsaids_training_adaptation \| 10.3389/fnut.2026.1850279] Medication need and dosing stay with a clinician or product directions, not this compiler.
- An athlete immune-health review warns against returning to exercise before infection symptoms have cleared because infection plus heavy exertion can carry additional risk. [C \| 13_vaccines_immunology/exercise_immunity_vaccines \| 10.1007/s40279-019-01160-3] **PLANNING SICK DAY:** no VO2 and no PR; keep scheduled protein and the 20:15 sleep window, use easy steps only as tolerated, and do not chase 10,000 through fever, dizziness, or systemic illness.

<a id="hc-page-033"></a>
### CHAPTER II.23 · PAGE 033 — 22. GLP-1 GI CARD

Nausea, diarrhea, and constipation are among the common gastrointestinal effects reported with tirzepatide, especially during dose escalation. [A \| 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins \| 10.1503/cmaj.250502] A SURMOUNT-1–4 post-hoc analysis confirms that appetite suppression and GI events can affect intake, while also noting that micronutrients were not systematically measured and that overt coded malnutrition events were uncommon in the trials. [B \| 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins \| 10.1016/j.obpill.2026.100248]

**PLANNING / GI — food handling, not medication treatment:**

- **Nausea or early fullness:** split a planned plate into two smaller sittings, begin with the protein portion, and stop at comfortable fullness. Do not force a giant 165 g catch-up meal at night.
- **Constipation:** change one whole-food fiber source at a time, increase it slowly, keep fluids accessible, and retain easy walking. The adult tirzepatide passages do not supply a personal fiber titration dose, so none is invented.
- **Diarrhea or vomiting:** pause experimental treats and large fatty meals, use simpler tolerated foods and fluids, and remove hard training. Persistent symptoms or inability to keep fluids down go to the prescribing clinician.
- **Sugar-alcohol desserts:** classify keto candies, bars, and large sugar-alcohol servings as rare while GI symptoms are active. This is a symptom-management planning default; the accepted adult evidence did not test a product-specific threshold.
- **Coffee:** attach it to the **05:50 breakfast** if fasted coffee triggers nausea; stop the coffee before deleting breakfast protein.
- Do not change tirzepatide dose, add an incretin, or create a laxative/antidiarrheal protocol here.

<a id="hc-page-034"></a>
### CHAPTER II.24 · PAGE 034 — 23. COFFEE FINE PRINT

- **Cold brew versus drip — PLANNING CAUTION:** the retrieved library did not establish which brew is stronger. Check the package or café caffeine amount; “cold brew” is not a dose. All caffeinated versions remain inside the **11:15 cutoff**.
- **Collagen coffee — PLANNING ARITHMETIC:** it counts only the protein grams shown on its label and does not replace the planned ≥40 g breakfast. The evidence-backed target is total daily high-quality protein distributed across feedings, not a beverage name. [A \| 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands \| 10.1007/s40279-025-02285-4]
- **Plant milk — PLANNING LABEL CHECK:** it is not automatically protein-equivalent to pasteurized dairy or ultrafiltered milk. Compare calories and protein on the label; use it as preference, not as a body-composition upgrade.
- **Decaf — PLANNING DEFAULT:** after 11:15 it may serve as a ritual only if it does not disturb the 20:15 wind-down; it does not authorize regular caffeinated coffee, energy drinks, or pre-workout.
- Added-sugar drinks remain the established dental concern in the accepted guidance. [A \| 06_hair_teeth/teeth_remineralization_caries \| 10.1007/s00784-026-06880-1] General dental evidence also identifies frequency and duration of acidic exposure as contributors to erosive wear, but it did not test ordinary coffee sipping. [C \| 06_hair_teeth/teeth_remineralization_caries \| 10.1186/s12903-025-05968-6] **PLANNING DENTAL CAUTION:** finish coffee with the breakfast window instead of sipping it across three hours; this is exposure-control logistics, not proof that black coffee caused erosion.
- **Raw-milk coffee remains prohibited.** Heating uncertainty and preference do not override the raw-dairy pathogen evidence. [C \| 01_food_inflammation/raw_milk_safety \| 10.36519/idcm.2026.984] [C \| 01_food_inflammation/raw_milk_safety \| 10.3389/fmicb.2026.1748367]

<a id="hc-page-035"></a>
### CHAPTER II.25 · PAGE 035 — 24. EATING OUT / ALCOHOL / NICOTINE / STIMS

**PLANNING DEFAULT — restaurant meal:** choose a recognizable protein entrée first; add rice, potatoes, bread, or fruit in proportion to the day's training; add a vegetable when available; stop at comfortable fullness on tirzepatide; and log the meal without punishment fasting or intervals. Restaurant placement after the long run is convenience, not a proven metabolic window.

**ALCOHOL COVERAGE GAP:** the named alcohol folder returned no usable human passage establishing a recovery-safe amount, an ideal pre-sleep interval, or an acceptable pre-long-run dose for him. **Single PLANNING DEFAULT:** alcohol is assumed off unless the profile declares use; no alcohol protocol is generated.

**PLANNING DISCLOSURE LINE:** nicotine, cannabis, and energy-drink use are absent from the injected profile, so they are assumed off. If any are used, declare product, dose, frequency, and time before recompiling interactions. Energy drinks count as caffeine and may not cross the locked **11:15** cutoff; the late-caffeine sleep finding remains applicable. [C \| 04_cognition_learning/caffeine \| 10.3389/fnut.2025.1659220]

### CHAPTER II.26 · PAGE 036 — 25. CARDIAC / BP GATE BEFORE INTENSIFYING

> **STOP THE QUALITY PROGRESSION:** do not self-start brutal Thursday repeats if exertion brings chest pain, fainting/presyncope, sustained palpitations, or unexpected shortness of breath. Retrieved cardiovascular guidance treats fatigue, exertional breathlessness, palpitations, and chest pain as symptoms requiring evaluation, while rhythm literature also recognizes presyncope/syncope among symptomatic presentations. [A \| 14_hormones_thyroid_heart/athlete_cardiac_health \| 10.1093/eurheartj/ehae176] [C \| 14_hormones_thyroid_heart/athlete_cardiac_health \| 10.1016/j.hrthm.2017.05.012] This is a stop gate, not a diagnosis or workup algorithm.

No personal BP reading was supplied. **PLANNING DEFAULT:** obtain a properly performed resting measurement before intensifying the quality session or adding any stimulant. If office readings are repeatedly concerning or inconsistent, clinician-directed home or ambulatory confirmation is more defensible than guessing from one reading. [C \| 14_hormones_thyroid_heart/blood_pressure_endothelium \| 10.3389/fcvm.2026.1834237] Do not use caffeine, tadalafil assumptions, heat exposure, or “athlete's heart” language to explain away symptoms.

| Food additions | Potatoes and rice | USE AS TRAINING CARBOHYDRATE; portion follows the day, not a food-ban list. |

| Food additions | Fruit carbohydrates: papaya, cherries, mango, melon, oranges | USE WHOLE FRUIT; it supplies carbohydrate without creating a special 'natural fructose' exemption. |

<a id="hc-page-039"></a>
### CHAPTER III.02 · PAGE 039 — 0. PURPOSE AND DECISION LANGUAGE

This module evaluates the requested food, home, spiritual, recovery, “detox,” light, frequency, and traditional-practice ideas without allowing a selected item to become an automatic recommendation. It is an evidence-and-safety layer over the existing operating week. It does not change tirzepatide, prescribe IV fluids, diagnose thyroid disease, or turn an animal experiment into proof of human benefit.

Verdicts mean:

- **KEEP / ADD:** useful enough and compatible with the existing plan.
- **OPTIONAL:** reasonable preference or a limited-evidence experiment that must not displace the foundations.
- **CONDITION-SPECIFIC:** potentially relevant only when a symptom, diagnosis, lab, or sport demand matches the evidence.
- **CLINICIAN-ONLY:** evaluation or delivery requires a licensed clinician; no home protocol follows.
- **SKIP / DO NOT USE:** unsupported for the claimed purpose, unsafe for self-use, or both.
- **PLANNING DEFAULT:** logistics, not a physiological claim.

#### Evidence ladder

1. Current public-health guidance, systematic reviews, and convergent controlled human trials control decisions.
2. One small human trial can justify **OPTIONAL/monitor**, not “proven.” Biomarker improvement is not automatically better performance, longer life, or disease treatment.
3. Animal or cell work is a **mechanism lead only**. It can move an item from “unknown” to “watchlist,” but never to a human dose, an ADD, or a replacement for care.
4. Testimonials, before/after photos, mechanistic diagrams, “ancient,” “frequency,” “detox,” and patent status are not efficacy evidence.
5. Any safety signal, interaction, contamination problem, or medical-delivery requirement can override interesting efficacy signals.

| “Antiseptic fiber” / natural binders | “Antiseptic fiber” is not a recognized diet class. Viscous/food fiber may support the existing fiber target; oral clay/charcoal-style binding is a different, interaction-prone claim. | **TRANSLATE TO FOOD FIBER.** Oats, beans if tolerated, fruit, vegetables, and the existing psyllium audit are legitimate; do not call them toxin extraction. | Increase one source at a time so GLP-1 GI effects remain interpretable. |
| Cooked mushrooms | Culinary mushrooms are ordinary foods; cooking and variety do not validate medicinal-mushroom or immunity claims. | **ADD AS FOOD IF ENJOYED.** Lion’s mane/reishi/etc. remain in the supplement evidence audit, not inferred from a grocery mushroom. | Frozen or pre-sliced cooked mushrooms reduce waste. Ventilate the kitchen. |
| Dark chocolate | A calorie-dense food, not a cardiovascular or nervous-system treatment in this plan. | **OPTIONAL MEASURED TREAT.** Place after a protein-containing meal; use the label to fit energy. | Buy individually portioned squares rather than an untracked bulk snack. |
| Fish eggs / roe / traditional foods | Useful food variety, but products differ in sodium, preparation, refrigeration, and nutrient content. | **OPTIONAL FOOD.** Verify label, cold chain, pasteurization/cooking advice, and allergy; do not call it “blood building.” | Small sealed jars minimize spoilage. Do not store opened roe beyond label directions. |
| “Blood-building” foods | Fatigue/anemia cannot be diagnosed from a food concept. Iron, B12, and folate are condition- and lab-specific in the supplement audit. | **TRANSLATE THE GOAL:** if anemia symptoms or risk exists, clinician assessment precedes organ meat or pills. No iron stack by intuition. | Housing type is irrelevant. |
| Exactly 200 g carbohydrate daily | No retrieved passage makes 200 g universally correct for every day of this hybrid week. | **DO NOT HARD-LOCK YET.** It is a candidate starting value only after total energy and protein are set. Track two weeks: performance, GI tolerance, hunger, and scale trend; move carbohydrates around sessions before adding supplements. | Same number logic in apartment or house; convenience foods do not change the target. |

#### Costco-first sourcing card

Availability and SKU names change, so purchase by specification rather than by a social-media brand list.

| Category | First source | Label/quality check | Apartment quantity | House quantity |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Milk/yogurt/cottage cheese | Costco refrigerated dairy, then a normal grocery store | “Pasteurized,” protein and calories per serving, date, lactose tolerance; no raw-farm substitute | One milk container and one tub at a time | Bulk only if the household finishes it before the date |
| Juice | Costco or grocery refrigerated/shelf-stable aisle | “100% juice,” pasteurized, no “juice drink” substitution; USDA Organic seal is optional production verification, not a health halo | Smallest container that prevents waste | Multi-pack only after actual weekly use is known |
| Poultry/fish/lean beef | Costco frozen/fresh, then grocery/butcher | Ingredient list, lean cut, package integrity, safe storage date | Frozen individual portions reduce spoilage | Batch portion and freeze immediately |
| Oysters | Reputable high-turnover seafood counter or sealed cooked/canned product | Keep cold; reject damaged/open packages; cook according to official guidance; never use smell as a pathogen test | Buy only the amount cooked that day | Same; a grill does not make raw oysters safe |
| Liver/organ meat | Inspected butcher or sealed frozen grocery product | Species/cut, package date, nutrition data when available; no glandular supplement substitution | Freeze small labeled portions | Same; freezer space is not permission for daily use |
| Eggs/roe | Costco/grocery refrigerated section | Shell/package integrity, refrigeration, handling/cooking directions, sodium for roe | Small package until use rate is proven | Larger package only with a dated use plan |
| Potatoes/rice/oats/fruit/vegetables | Costco when freezer/pantry space exists; grocery for perishable variety | Plain ingredient list; frozen produce is acceptable; no detox/organic requirement | Frozen fruit/veg, microwave rice, and smaller fresh-fruit rotation | Bulk sacks/trays only if dry/cool storage prevents waste/pests |
| Honey/dark chocolate/treat inputs | Normal grocery or Costco | Single-ingredient honey if desired; nutrition label still controls amount; no medicinal purity claim | Pre-portion immediately | Store bulk out of the default eating path |
| Olive/coconut oil and butter | Grocery/Costco | Named oil, sealed/date, storage directions; avoid proprietary “metabolic fat” blends | One default unsaturated oil; smallest flavor-fat container | Same default; bulk only if turnover protects quality |
| Bone broth/gelatin/collagen | Grocery/Costco | Protein and sodium per serving, ingredient list, third-party supplement check if sold as a powder | Shelf-stable cartons/single powder | Homemade is optional only when time and food-safety handling are realistic |
| Grass/sod tray | Local garden center plus building-approved waterproof planter/tray | Confirm sod has not been treated with something incompatible with skin contact; verify drainage, weight, insects, and lease rules | Start with one small removable tray | A clean lawn area usually makes a purchased tray unnecessary |
| Natural-fiber clothing | Any retailer with a real fiber-content/care label | Buy for fit, chafing control, climate, and durability—not “Hz” claims | One tested garment before replacing a wardrobe | Same |
| Supplements | Costco/pharmacy only after the supplement-audit verdict | Exact ingredient/form/amount, lot, expiration, and live NSF/USP/other reputable certification lookup; avoid proprietary blends | One item trial at a time | Same; house storage is not a reason to stockpile |

<a id="hc-page-041"></a>
### CHAPTER III.04 · PAGE 041 — 2. PLANNING DEFAULT — HOW THESE FOODS ENTER THE EXISTING MEAL PLAN

These are substitutions, not an additional diet layered on top of the Costco plan.

| Slot | Base choice | Allowed addition/swap | Guardrail |
|---|---|---|---|
| 05:50 breakfast | Existing ≥40 g complete-protein meal | Pasteurized milk/yogurt; oats; banana, mango, papaya, berries; a small serving of 100% juice | Coffee remains with/after breakfast and caffeine ends by 11:15. Honey is counted, not free. |
| 11:00 desk meal | Poultry, fish, lean meat, eggs, dairy or other complete-protein vehicle | Potato/rice, carrots, cooked mushrooms, fruit | Protein first on low-appetite days. Do not replace this meal with juice, bone broth, or collagen coffee. |
| Pre-17:00 session | Existing tolerated carb/protein plan | Potato/rice, fruit, small juice or measured honey | Match the amount to the session. Tuesday easy work does not require a sugar ritual; Saturday long-run fueling follows the main plan. |
| Post-session/dinner | Complete protein + planned carbohydrate + vegetables | Cooked oysters as variety, dairy, potatoes/rice, mushrooms, carrots | Creatine remains 5 g. Coconut/butter are flavor fats, not recovery compounds. |
| Treat | Existing planned treat allocation | Yogurt-cherry bark, banana-whey ice cream, measured honey, dark chocolate | Treat replaces other energy; it does not trigger fasting or punishment cardio. |
| Weekly variety | Fish/lean meat rotation | Small occasional liver or roe | Not daily; verify vitamin A for liver and refrigeration/sodium for roe. |

**Two-week adjustment rule:** change one food variable at a time, keep the existing protein/creatine/sleep/training anchors, and record the effect. A food stays when it improves adherence or tolerance without worsening GI symptoms or displacing protein. It leaves when it reliably worsens symptoms, crowds out core foods, or depends on an unsupported treatment claim.

<a id="hc-page-042"></a>
### CHAPTER III.05 · PAGE 042 — 3. WATER QUALITY AND SOURCING

Dallas Water Utilities’ 2025 Consumer Confidence Report says the municipal system met or exceeded applicable state and federal requirements and lists detected contaminants. That report—not a pitcher advertisement—is the starting point. [City of Dallas 2025 Water Quality Report](https://dallascityhall.com/departments/waterutilities/PublishingImages/pages/water_quality_reports/2025%20Water%20Quality%20Report.pdf) EPA explains how to use Consumer Confidence Reports. [EPA CCR guide](https://www.epa.gov/ccr)

#### Decision path

1. Identify the actual address/provider and read its current CCR. Apartment residents should also ask management about building age, recent plumbing work, and any building-specific testing; a city report does not test the final faucet.
2. Decide the problem: taste/chlorine, a specific health contaminant, hardness/scale, or emergency reliability. Do not buy “alkaline,” “structured,” “EZ,” or “high-vibration” water before naming the problem.
3. Select a filter certified for the exact reduction claim. NSF certification directories let buyers verify models rather than trust a logo screenshot. [NSF product certification](https://www.nsf.org/nutrition-wellness/product-and-ingredient-certification)
4. Verify the model number in the certifier’s live database, replacement interval, cartridge cost, flow rate, and whether performance requires professional installation.
5. Retest only when a building-specific concern, private well, official notice, or clinician/public-health reason makes it useful. A home “TDS” meter does not identify lead, pathogens, PFAS, or health safety.

| Setting | Good default | Upgrade only when justified | Avoid |
|---|---|---|---|
| Apartment | Cold tap water after normal flushing; certified countertop/pitcher filter if taste or a matched reduction claim warrants it; leak tray under any device | Under-sink system only with landlord approval, shutoff access, and leak protection | Unapproved plumbing changes, permanent RO waste line, mineral-drop stacks, storing huge emergency jugs beyond floor/storage limits |
| House on municipal water | Same CCR-first method; certified point-of-use filter | Whole-house equipment only after testing identifies a whole-house problem; use licensed plumbing/electrical work | Treating hardness as toxicity; buying filtration from a salesperson’s unverified home demo |
| House on private well | **Different gate:** periodic testing through state/local public-health guidance is necessary because no municipal CCR covers the well | Treatment matched to laboratory results | Assuming clear/tasty water is safe |

**Minerals:** label mineral content may affect taste and dietary totals, but it does not make premium bottled water medically superior. Safe, accessible water that supports the established hydration plan wins. “EZ/structured water” remains a marketing claim unless a reproducible human clinical outcome and product verification appear.

<a id="hc-page-043"></a>
### CHAPTER III.06 · PAGE 043 — 4. APARTMENT VERSUS HOUSE PRACTICE CARD

| Practice | Apartment version | House version | Evidence boundary / safety |
|---|---|---|---|
| Balcony grass patch | **PLANNING DEFAULT:** use a shallow waterproof tray with removable real sod, drainage capture, conservative dimensions, and no runoff over the balcony. Confirm lease/HOA, balcony load, waterproofing, pest, and fire rules first. Use it as an outdoor cue for prayer, light, or quiet standing. | Use a clean, inspected lawn area; avoid pesticides, sharp objects, animal waste, and extreme surface temperatures. | A planter isolated from the ground is not proof of electrical “earthing.” Grounding research is small and does not clear the strong-evidence gate. [PubMed grounding review](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31831261/) |
| Bare feet / grounding | Barefoot only on a clean private surface. Shoes on public/shared grass are reasonable. | Same foot-safety rule. | **OPTIONAL RITUAL, NOT MEDICAL TREATMENT.** Benefits may come from being outdoors, pausing, or walking; do not attribute them to electrons. |
| Morning light | Existing 04:35–04:50 outdoor block on balcony/walkway; view the sky, do not stare at the sun. | Porch/yard version, same clock. | Morning light is a circadian timing tool; tanning is not required. Light therapy timing can shift circadian phase. [NHLBI circadian treatment](https://www.nhlbi.nih.gov/health/circadian-rhythm-disorders/treatment) |
| Circadian lighting | Bright normal household light after waking; dimmer/warm ordinary lights during 19:15 wind-down; cover glaring LEDs. | Same; smart bulbs are optional convenience. | No need for “full-spectrum frequency baths.” Keep the locked sleep window. |
| Sunbathing / abdomen sun | Do not schedule tanning. Outdoor light/movement can happen with shade and sun protection when UV is elevated. | Same. | CDC states a tan is skin injury and excess UV increases skin-cancer risk; abdomen exposure has no special established benefit. [CDC sun safety](https://www.cdc.gov/skin-cancer/prevention/index.html) |
| Slow breathwork | Chair, floor, or balcony; 5 minutes of comfortable slow breathing during wind-down or prayer. | Same, indoors or outdoors. | Human reviews find autonomic/HRV changes, but outcomes and methods vary. Stop if dizzy; no prolonged breath holds in water or while driving. [Slow-breathing meta-analysis](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/35623448/) |
| Prayer | 5–10 minutes attached to morning light or 19:15 wind-down; use it for worship/reflection, not as a substitute for care. | Same; a dedicated chair/desk is optional. | Faith practice is included on values, meaning, and spiritual grounds. This compiler does not claim prayer cures disease. |
| Knees-over-toes work | No sled required: supported backward walking in a clear hallway or on a powered-off treadmill belt only if safe, tibialis raises against a wall, calf raises, and graded split-squat range. | A light backward sled drag may be available, but load and surface remain progressive. | “Knees over toes” is not inherently forbidden or a cure. Evidence supports graded hip-and-knee exercise for patellofemoral pain; backward-walking findings are largely in knee OA/rehab populations, not proof of prevention in a healthy runner. [Hip/knee exercise review](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34079359/), [backward-walking review](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40924430/) |
| Cold exposure | A normal cool shower is the simplest option; avoid heavy tubs on balconies and unmanaged water spills. | A plunge requires drainage, sanitation, safe access, and GFCI-compliant equipment installed for wet environments. Never use improvised electrical cooling near water. | **OPTIONAL, NOT REQUIRED.** A 2025 review found time-dependent signals but few trials and limited population diversity. Cold can acutely raise BP; anyone with cardiovascular symptoms/risk needs medical advice. [Cold-water systematic review](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39879231/), [cardiovascular meta-analysis](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38663342/) |
| Natural-fiber clothing | Cotton/linen/wool are comfort and preference options; follow fire-safety and care labels. | Same. | No “00 Hz,” healing resonance, or endocrine-treatment claim is established. Do not discard functional athletic synthetics if they prevent chafing. |
| Low-clutter / feng shui | Use as a room-layout framework: clear walking route, dedicated sleep zone, charging outside reach, training gear stored visibly. | Same, with more zones. | **PLANNING/BEHAVIOR DESIGN**, not energy medicine. Keep only observable goals: fewer trip hazards, less clutter, easier routines. |
| Endocrine-exposure reduction | Damp-dust/mop, ventilate cooking, avoid heating food in damaged/non-heat-rated containers, wash hands, and reduce unnecessary fragranced products if sensitive. | Same; maintain HVAC/filters and store pesticides per label. | NIEHS says exposures cannot be completely eliminated; use proportionate steps rather than a “detox house.” [NIEHS fact sheet](https://www.niehs.nih.gov/health/materials/endocrine_disruptors_508.pdf), [FDA food-contact overview](https://www.fda.gov/food/food-ingredients-packaging/food-packaging-other-substances-come-contact-food-information-consumers) |
| Sound bath / cymatics | Music or sound may be used for relaxation at safe volume and neighbor-respectful hours. | Same. | **NO CELLULAR-HEALING CLAIM.** Acoustic patterns in a medium do not establish disease treatment. |
| Red light / PBM | Do not confuse red ambient bulbs with a medical/light device. If considered, record exact cleared indication, wavelength/device, eye precautions, and cost. | Same. | Reviews do not show whole-body PBM improves exercise performance/recovery; localized results are mixed and device-specific. [Whole-body PBM review](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/39883205/), [functional-performance review](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/38150056/) |
| Radionics/biofrequency device | No purchase or self-treatment protocol. | No purchase or self-treatment protocol. | **SKIP.** Frequency-healing disease claims require controlled human evidence; FTC has acted against unsupported frequency/device treatment claims. [FTC warning example](https://www.ftc.gov/system/files/warning-letters/covid-19-letter_to_bioenergywellness.pdf) |

#### PLANNING DEFAULT — graded knee accessory block

This is for a pain-free user and is not rehabilitation. Add **one 8–10 minute block twice weekly**, preferably before Monday lower lifting and during Sunday mobility: 3–5 minutes easy backward walking on a clear flat surface, 2 × 12–15 tibialis raises, 2 × 10–15 calf raises, and 2 × 6–8 supported split squats per side at a comfortable range. Progress range before load. Stop and seek assessment for swelling, locking, giving way, sharp pain, gait change, or worsening symptoms the next day. Do not turn Tuesday/Thursday easy aerobic sessions into extra heavy leg days.

[FDA homeopathic products](https://www.fda.gov/drugs/information-drug-class/homeopathic-products) | “Highly diluted” does not demonstrate efficacy; an arsenic-named product is not a safer substitute for diagnosis/treatment. |

Oral food/fluid is the default unless a clinician identifies a medical indication. |

[FDA bentonite warning](https://www.fda.gov/drugs/drug-safety-and-availability/la-fda-advierte-los-consumidores-sobre-los-riesgos-que-la-arcilla-de-bentonita-de-alikay-naturals) | A “binder” must identify the toxin, exposure test, clinical indication, and validated treatment. Food fiber is not interchangeable with chelation or emergency toxicology. |
| Coffee enema | **DO NOT USE.** A systematic review found no effectiveness study and nine adverse-event case reports, including colitis and more critical events. [Coffee-enema systematic review](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32899046/) | No home recipe, temperature, volume, retention time, or equipment list is provided. Drinking coffee within the caffeine rules is a different exposure. |
| Liver/gallbladder flush | **DO NOT USE.** Human detox/cleanse evidence is low quality and colon/cleanse procedures can be harmful. [NCCIH detoxes and cleanses](https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/detoxes-and-cleanses-what-you-need-to-know) | It does not diagnose or treat gallstones, fatty liver, hepatitis, or obstruction. Symptoms require medical evaluation. |
| Castor oil / castor packs | **COVERAGE GAP FOR DETOX OR SYSTEMIC HEALING; SKIP THAT CLAIM.** | A topical cosmetic use and an ingested laxative are different products and risks. The selector must record the exact intended use before any evidence card; no blanket protocol. |
| Digestive enzymes | **CONDITION-SPECIFIC.** Enzymes are not interchangeable; benefit depends on a defined deficiency/intolerance and product (for example, lactase is not pancreatic enzyme replacement). | On tirzepatide, nausea/fullness is not proof of enzyme deficiency. Persistent GI symptoms go to the prescriber/clinician before a stack. |
| Betaine HCl / hydrochloric acid | **CLINICIAN-GATED; NO SELF-TEST.** **COVERAGE GAP** for routine use in this user. | Reflux, ulcers, medications, and actual cause matter. A warming/burning sensation is not a diagnostic endpoint. |
| Chlorophyll/chlorophyllin drops | A systematic review exists but the retrieved abstract does not establish a relevant cut, performance, or “blood cleansing” outcome. [Chlorophyll review](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24670123/) | **SKIP FOR CURRENT GOALS.** Green vegetables do not require a chlorophyll supplement story. |
| Spore-based probiotics | Small, strain-specific RCTs report symptom changes in defined functional-GI populations, but one *Bacillus* blend cannot be generalized to every spore product or a symptom-free user. [Functional dyspepsia pilot RCT](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34358486/), [constipation RCT](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34119240/) | **OPTIONAL ONLY FOR A DEFINED GI TARGET AND EXACT STRAIN.** NCCIH notes product/strain uncertainty and limited safety data. [NCCIH probiotics](https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/probiotics-usefulness-and-safety) |
| Black seed oil / *Nigella sativa* | Meta-analyses report changes in inflammatory and lipid biomarkers, but an umbrella review rated most evidence low or very low quality. [Inflammation meta-analysis](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37036558/), [umbrella review](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/37057067/) | **OPTIONAL/LOW PRIORITY, NOT AN AUTOMATIC ADD.** No retrieved proof it improves his running, strength retention, or meaningful cardiovascular outcomes. Interaction/product-quality review still applies. |
| CoQ10 | NCCIH finds heart-disease/heart-failure findings inconclusive, no meaningful BP effect, and no overall support for statin muscle pain; interactions can occur with warfarin and insulin. [NCCIH CoQ10](https://www.nccih.nih.gov/health/coenzyme-q10) | **SKIP FOR CURRENT GOALS** unless a specific clinician-reviewed indication is recorded. |
| Chanca piedra / *Phyllanthus niruri* | Limited human evidence is specific to kidney stones and sometimes adjunctive to established urologic treatment. [2025 systematic review](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/40480426/), [2020 meta-analysis](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32333735/) | **NOT A LIVER CLEANSE.** CLINICIAN-ONLY consideration for documented nephrolithiasis; no preventive home stack for an unconfirmed condition. |
| Artichoke leaf | Meta-analyses report modest lipid-marker reductions in hyperlipidemic populations. [2021 meta-analysis](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/34569671/) AHA’s current patient guidance does not recommend supplements as cholesterol management. [AHA cholesterol management](https://www.heart.org/en/health-topics/cholesterol/prevention-and-treatment-of-high-cholesterol-hyperlipidemia) | **OPTIONAL/CLINICIAN REVIEW ONLY IF LIPIDS ARE ACTUALLY ABNORMAL.** It is not a liver flush and does not replace established risk assessment. |
| Quercetin | The main supplement audit performs item-specific local retrieval. | **LOW PRIORITY UNTIL A DEFINED OUTCOME CLEARS HUMAN EVIDENCE AND INTERACTION REVIEW.** Do not add it merely because it is a polyphenol. |
| Magnesium | Already present in the supplement catalog with an intake/deficiency/kidney-function gate. | **DO NOT DUPLICATE.** The selector records interest; the supplement audit controls the verdict. |
| Dr. Broda Barnes / basal temperature | Historical interest does not replace current thyroid diagnosis. The American Thyroid Association publishes clinical guidelines for hypothyroidism. [ATA guidelines](https://www.thyroid.org/professionals/ata-professional-guidelines/) | **HISTORY ONLY.** Do not diagnose low thyroid from temperature, cholesterol, fatigue, or weight alone; no T3/thyroid-hormone protocol. |
| “High cholesterol means stress/low metabolic energy” | **REJECT AS A COMPLETE EXPLANATION.** Stress can affect behavior and BP, but high LDL contributes to atherosclerosis and can reflect diet, genetics, conditions, or medications. [AHA cholesterol guide](https://www.heart.org/en/health-topics/cholesterol/your-complete-guide-to-understanding-cholesterol-and-lipids), [AHA stress](https://www.heart.org/en/healthy-living/healthy-lifestyle/stress-management/stress-and-heart-health) | Measure and interpret a lipid panel in clinical context. Do not wave away LDL, start thyroid hormone, or use supplement “flushes.” |
| EZ water / structured water | **NO CLINICALLY VALIDATED PRODUCT OUTCOME FOUND.** | **SKIP THE PREMIUM CLAIM.** Verify potable-water safety and filter certification instead. |
| Linen “00 Hz” fabric | **NO DEFINED, REPRODUCIBLE HEALTH EXPOSURE OR HUMAN OUTCOME FOUND.** | **PREFERENCE ONLY.** Buy linen for comfort/durability, not a medical frequency number. |
| Radionics / full-spectrum frequency bath / cellular cymatics | **NO RELIABLE HUMAN CLINICAL BASIS FOUND FOR DISEASE OR CELLULAR HEALING.** FTC requires competent and reliable evidence for treatment claims and has challenged unsupported frequency-device marketing. [FTC warning](https://www.ftc.gov/system/files/warning-letters/covid-19-letter_to_bioenergywellness.pdf) | **SKIP AS TREATMENT.** Music, colored light, and room design may remain art/relaxation preferences without medical claims. |

<a id="hc-page-045"></a>
### CHAPTER III.08 · PAGE 045 — 6. FAITH, BIBLE, AND THE HISTORICAL JESUS

This module keeps three questions separate:

1. **Historical:** what can standard historical methods infer about Jesus of Nazareth and the early movement?
2. **Textual/theological:** what do the New Testament and Christian tradition claim about who Jesus is?
3. **Spiritual practice:** how will prayer, Scripture, worship, and community shape daily life?

They overlap, but historical evidence does not experimentally prove divinity or resurrection; those are theological claims received in faith. Conversely, saying a claim is theological does not mean the underlying texts or historical people are imaginary.

#### Primary-source evidence map

| Source | What it establishes | Limitation |
|---|---|---|
| Paul’s First Letter to the Corinthians, especially 15:1–11 | An early Christian witness records a received proclamation of Jesus’ death, burial, resurrection, named appearances, and Paul’s own experience. USCCB notes the formula is older than Paul’s wording. [USCCB 1 Corinthians 15](https://bible.usccb.org/bible/1corinthians/15) | It is an insider Christian source and theological testimony, not a neutral modern report. |
| Synoptic Gospels and John | Multiple ancient narrative witnesses to Jesus’ ministry, teaching, death, and claimed resurrection. Catholic official teaching calls the Gospels the principal witness to Jesus’ life and teaching. [Vatican *Dei Verbum* 18–19](https://www.vatican.va/archive/hist_councils/ii_vatican_council/documents/vat-ii_const_19651118_dei-verbum_en.html) | They are theological ancient biographies written for communities; genre, authorship, sources, and differences need interpretation. |
| Josephus, *Antiquities* 20.9.1 | A first-century Jewish historian refers to James as the brother of “Jesus who was called Christ.” [Josephus text](https://openscriptorium.com/read/josephus/027-antiquities-book-20) | Josephus’ longer passage in Book 18 contains disputed/interpolated wording. Do not present every received sentence as equally secure. |
| Tacitus, *Annals* 15.44 | A non-Christian Roman historian reports that “Christus” was executed under Pontius Pilate during Tiberius’ reign and that Christians existed in Rome under Nero. [Perseus/Tufts primary text](https://www.perseus.tufts.edu/hopper/text.jsp?doc=Perseus%3Atext%3A1999.02.0077%3Abook%3D15%3Achapter%3D44) | Written decades later, hostile in tone, and not an eyewitness account of Jesus’ ministry. |
| Acts | Narrates the early Jesus movement from Jerusalem outward and the roles of Peter and Paul. [USCCB Acts introduction](https://bible.usccb.org/bible/acts/0) | It is a Christian theological history and should be compared with Paul’s letters and ancient context. |

**Responsible conclusion:** the combined early Christian and non-Christian source base makes Jesus’ historical existence, association with the early movement, and execution under Pontius Pilate the well-supported historical core. The exact reconstruction of every saying/event and the theological meaning of the resurrection require further argument; the report will not pretend that Tacitus or Josephus independently proves every Christian doctrine.

#### Official-document learning path

- **Bible text and notes:** [USCCB Bible](https://bible.usccb.org/bible), a stable official Catholic publication with introductions and notes.
- **How Catholics read Scripture:** [USCCB, Understanding the Bible](https://www.usccb.org/bible/understanding-the-bible).
- **Scripture, tradition, inspiration, and Gospel historicity:** Vatican II, [*Dei Verbum*](https://www.vatican.va/archive/hist_councils/ii_vatican_council/documents/vat-ii_const_19651118_dei-verbum_en.html).
- **Primary non-Christian texts:** Tacitus and Josephus above. Read the passage itself before an apologetics summary.
- **Community verification:** take unresolved doctrinal questions to a qualified pastor/priest and a church Bible study or catechesis program. Record the church/tradition because “official” means different authorities in Catholic, Orthodox, and Protestant communities.

#### Eight-week Jesus curriculum — PLANNING DEFAULT

Use **15 minutes at 19:15** during wind-down, with the phone in focus mode: 10 minutes reading, 3 minutes written observation, 2 minutes prayer. This does not move the 20:15 bed. Sunday may use 30 minutes after meal prep.

| Week | Reading | Study question | Verification task |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Mark | What does Jesus do and claim? | Mark repeated themes; distinguish text from your interpretation. |
| 2 | Luke 1–12 | How does Luke frame Jesus in Israel’s story? | Read Luke’s prologue and note its stated method/purpose. |
| 3 | Luke 13–24 | What leads to the crucifixion and resurrection claim? | Compare the passion/resurrection sequence with Mark. |
| 4 | Acts 1–12 | How does the earliest community preach Jesus? | List speeches, audiences, and recurring claims. |
| 5 | Acts 13–28 + Galatians | How do mission and controversy develop? | Compare Acts’ Paul with Paul’s own letter. |
| 6 | John | How does John’s portrait differ in structure and emphasis? | Separate shared historical anchors from Johannine theology. |
| 7 | 1 Corinthians, especially ch. 15 | What did Paul say he received and pass on? | Use the USCCB notes; date the letter relative to later sources. |
| 8 | James + Tacitus 15.44 + Josephus 20.9.1 + *Dei Verbum* 11–19 | What is historical evidence, what is doctrine, and what is practice? | Write one page with three headings: history / theology / discipleship. |

**Daily prayer cue — PLANNING DEFAULT:** morning light: one minute of gratitude and the Lord’s Prayer; 19:15: read, write one sentence (“What does this show about Jesus?”), pray one concrete response; Sunday: worship/community when available. Prayer is not scored as a biohack and does not replace treatment.

<a id="hc-page-046"></a>
### CHAPTER III.09 · PAGE 046 — 7. SOURCE AND PRODUCT VERIFICATION CHECKLIST

For every product, paper, video, historical claim, or “doctor says” recommendation:

1. **Define it:** exact ingredient/species/strain/form, intended outcome, population, route, and time horizon. “Magnesium,” “probiotic,” “light,” and “banana water” are not precise interventions.
2. **Find the primary record:** DOI/PubMed/ClinicalTrials.gov for science; FDA/CDC/NIH/EPA/AHA or professional guideline for safety; primary ancient text for history; the relevant church’s own document for doctrine.
3. **Check study type and result:** human randomized result > observational > animal > cells > theory. A protocol has no outcome. A review with mostly tiny biased trials stays weak.
4. **Check fit:** age, health status, training, medication, deficiency, dose/form, comparator, duration, and actual endpoint. Disease-population biomarker effects are indirect for a healthy 26-year-old athlete.
5. **De-duplicate:** the same DOI in multiple folders, articles, or influencer posts counts once.
6. **Separate surrogate from outcome:** LDL, CRP, HRV, microbiome abundance, “detox markers,” and antioxidant capacity are not automatically performance, symptoms, or longevity.
7. **Audit conflicts and certainty:** industry funding is not automatic disqualification, but formulation-specific claims, selective outcomes, high heterogeneity, and publication bias lower confidence.
8. **Verify the product:** FDA does not pre-approve supplements for safety/effectiveness. [FDA supplement regulation](https://www.fda.gov/consumers/consumer-updates/fda-101-dietary-supplements) Prefer a live third-party directory such as [NSF Certified for Sport](https://nsfsport-prod.nsf.org/certified-products/index.php), exact lot/model identity, single ingredients, and transparent amounts. A certificate supplied only by the seller is insufficient.
9. **Check current warnings:** FDA warning letters/recalls, interaction databases through a pharmacist, and sport banned-substance rules where applicable.
10. **Predefine the decision:** target metric, trial length, what stays constant, stop signals, and keep/remove threshold. Trial one optional item at a time.

#### Animal-evidence gate

Animal data may justify watching an item when there is a plausible human goal, known exposure, reproducible effect across independent laboratories, and adequate toxicology. It still receives **ANIMAL-ONLY / NOT HUMAN-PROVEN**, no personal dose conversion, no stack placement, and no claim that a mouse mechanism will occur in a 230 lb human. Human safety absence is not safety proof. The next action is search for registered/peer-reviewed human trials—not self-experimenting with research chemicals.

<a id="hc-page-047"></a>
### CHAPTER III.10 · PAGE 047 — 8. CURRENT-PLAN CHANGE CONTROL

The final report may change the current plan only through these gates:

| Finding | Allowed change | Not allowed |
|---|---|---|
| Selected ordinary food fits protein/energy/GI needs | Swap it into an existing meal slot | Add it on top of all calories because it is “natural” |
| Heat or housing makes outdoor work impractical | Indoor walk/bike, normal circadian-light logistics | Skip movement or buy a frequency device |
| Defined GI symptom plus strain-specific human evidence | One monitored food/probiotic change; prescriber contact if persistent | Generic enzyme/HCl/probiotic pile |
| Clinician-confirmed deficiency/condition | Use clinician plan and update the audit | Infer a deficiency from race, fatigue, temperature, or a social-media symptom list |
| Human evidence is weak but safety reasonable | Optional one-at-a-time trial with a recorded outcome | Promote to required daily stack |
| Animal-only/cell-only evidence | Watchlist/research question | Human dose, peptide protocol, or therapeutic claim |
| Unsupported but harmless preference | Keep as comfort, worship, art, or organization | Rename it medicine or replace care |
| Safety concern or no plausible evidence | Remove/skip and document why | “Balance” the verdict with testimonials |

#### What changes now

- **Add:** the Bible/prayer learning block; the CCR-first water check; selectable ordinary food substitutions; safe apartment/home setup guidance; an optional short graded knee accessory block; the product/source verification checklist.
- **Keep:** 165 g Phase-A protein, creatine 5 g, morning outdoor light, the 04:30/20:15 clock, three progressive lifts, Tuesday easy aerobic work, Thursday single quality slot, Saturday long run, step floors, and Sunday prep.
- **Do not auto-add:** 200 g carbohydrate, extra supplements, extra hard leg work on easy-run days, liver/organ meat daily, coconut oil as the default fat, cold plunge, PBM, probiotics, black seed oil, CoQ10, quercetin, artichoke, chanca piedra, enzymes, or HCl.
- **Remove/refuse:** raw milk, raw oysters, home IVs, Arsenicum album, oral bentonite detox, coffee enemas, liver flushes, radionics/frequency-healing devices, EZ-water premiums, “00 Hz” clothing claims, tissue-cleansing claims, and the claim that elevated LDL is merely stress or low metabolic energy.

<a id="hc-page-048"></a>
### CHAPTER III.11 · PAGE 048 — 9. RED-FLAG STOP RULES

- Chest pain, fainting, sustained palpitations, unexpected exertional breathlessness, neurologic symptoms, or severe allergic reaction: stop the experiment/session and seek appropriate urgent medical evaluation.
- Persistent vomiting/diarrhea, inability to keep fluids down, severe or persistent abdominal pain, or dehydration concern on tirzepatide: contact the prescribing clinician; do not self-start IV fluids, enzymes, HCl, binders, or a flush.
- Suspected poisoning/heavy-metal exposure: contact Poison Control/public health/clinician; do not use clay, charcoal, coffee enemas, or OTC chelation.
- New knee swelling, locking, giving way, traumatic injury, or gait-changing pain: stop the branded knee routine and obtain qualified assessment.
- Cold exposure: never combine with alcohol, breath holds, unsupervised open water, improvised electrical equipment, or a balcony tub whose load/drainage has not been approved.
- Faith practices support meaning and community; they do not delay medical care or become proof that symptoms are moral/spiritual failure.

<a id="hc-page-049"></a>
### CHAPTER III.12 · PAGE 049 — 10. COVERAGE GAPS TO RESOLVE IN THE NEXT AUDIT

- Exact meaning/ingredients of “butter stick,” “banana water,” “milk and tissues cleaning,” “natural digestive aids,” “natural binders,” “man illumination,” and “nervous system disruptors.”
- Current apartment lease/balcony load and drainage rules; grass-patch dimensions cannot be selected safely without them.
- Actual Dallas address/provider and any building-specific plumbing concern; the citywide CCR cannot inspect the final faucet.
- Current lipid panel, BP, GI symptoms, kidney-stone history, liver/gallbladder diagnosis, allergies, and all medications; none may be inferred.
- Exact faith tradition and desired official authority (Catholic, Orthodox, or Protestant); the cited Vatican/USCCB documents are explicitly Catholic.
- Product-specific certificates/lots for any proposed supplement, probiotic strain, filter model, PBM device, or specialty food.
- Human outcome evidence for EZ/structured water, linen-frequency claims, radionics, frequency baths, cellular cymatics, special abdominal sunlight, milk tissue-cleansing, and generalized castor-oil detox was not established; these gaps currently prevent recommendation rather than invite guesswork.

<a id="hc-page-052"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.03 · PAGE 052 — 2. RESEARCH QUESTIONS USED FOR EACH CANDIDATE

For every selected item, the audit asks: What outcomes and effect magnitudes were reported? Which population was studied? What form, amount, and duration did the human studies use? Do independent human papers converge? What safety or interaction evidence was retrieved? How directly does that evidence fit the recorded problems, medications, and deficiencies? Every generated answer is tied to the references printed beside it.

<a id="hc-page-053"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.04 · PAGE 053 — 3. DECISION SYSTEM

- **STRONG:** at least two unique on-topic retrieved A/B human sources; **WEAK:** one; **NONE:** zero.
- Duplicate DOI hardlinks count once. General-adult evidence is distinguished from older-only evidence.
- The actionable shortlist is capped at **three new OTC items beyond creatine and whey-as-food**.
- Deficiency-dependent nutrients remain behind tests, diet review, or clinician review even when coverage is strong.
- “Retrieved studies used” describes research; it is not a personal dose.
- User-selected weak or sparse topics remain visible, receive priority deep review, and enter the consideration/watchlist; selection does not upgrade coverage or automatically place them in the active stack.

<a id="hc-page-055"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.06 · PAGE 055 — 5. DEFICIENCY, MEDICATION, AND SAFETY GATES

- **Vitamin D3:** Test 25(OH)D or establish clinician-documented deficiency; do not assume from skin tone or indoor work.
- **Magnesium:** Intake/deficiency and kidney-function gate; not an automatic sleep supplement.
- **Zinc:** Deficiency/intake gate; chronic excess and copper balance require review.
- **Iron if deficient:** CBC/iron studies and clinician review required; never a blind add.
- **Vitamin B12 if deficient:** Diet/lab/clinical deficiency gate.
- **Folate / methylfolate if deficient:** Diet/lab/clinical deficiency gate; assess B12 context.
- **Calcium with vitamin D if intake is low:** Estimate dietary calcium and assess vitamin D status before considering pills.
- **Psyllium / viscous fiber:** Increase only as tolerated; medication timing and GLP-1 GI symptoms must be considered.
- **Berberine:** Medication/GI review required, especially with an active glucose-lowering prescription.
- **Probiotics:** Strain-, condition-, and symptom-specific; not one interchangeable product class.
- **Green tea extract / EGCG:** Extract safety differs from brewed tea; liver-risk passages control the verdict.
- **Mucuna pruriens:** Dopaminergic/medication interaction review required.
- **5-HTP:** Serotonergic medication and mental-health review required.
- **CBD:** Medication, sedation, product-quality, and legal/workplace review required.
