### CHAPTER IV.12 · PAGE 061 — 7. QUESTION-BY-QUESTION DEEP EVIDENCE ANSWERS

<a id="hc-page-062"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.13 · PAGE 062 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Caffeine?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 6 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Caffeine ingestion significantly reduces cycling completion time and enhances mean power output, with no significant effect on heart rate or RPE; performance benefits observed across sex and training status, particularly in high-intensity intermittent exercise (HIIE). [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1745472] [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fphys.2026.1858094]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Caffeine doses ranged from 3.0 to 6.0 mg/kg, commonly administered 60 min pre-exercise in capsule or beverage form; low dose (~3 mg/kg) may offer optimal balance of efficacy and tolerability. [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fphys.2026.1858094] [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fphys.2026.1763606]
- **Mechanism:** Caffeine enhances central nervous system drive and neuromuscular recruitment, improving performance in high-intensity intermittent efforts; synergistic with creatine and β-alanine for explosive power and sustained effort. [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fphys.2026.1763606] [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fphys.2026.1858094]
- **Safety/interactions:** Adverse events include tachycardia, headache, insomnia, and increased energy; higher rates in caffeine group vs. placebo (p < 0.05); long-term use in adolescents linked to elevated BP, sleep disruption, and metabolic risk. [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1766993] [C | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.7759/cureus.103855]
- **Applicability:** Caffeine is effective for trained athletes in cycling and HIIE, particularly in sports with extreme rest/work ratios; benefits apply across sex and training status, with capsule and beverage forms showing efficacy. [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1745472] [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fphys.2026.1858094]
- **Convergence:** Two independent meta-analyses (S1, S4) both report significant ergogenic effects of caffeine on cycling and HIIE performance, with consistent findings across sex and training status, supporting convergence. [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1745472] [A | 04_cognition_learning/caffeine | 10.3389/fphys.2026.1858094]
- **Bottom line:** KEEP / REVIEW AGAINST EVIDENCE. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-063"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.14 · PAGE 063 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Creatine monohydrate?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 5 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Creatine monohydrate supplementation with resistance training improves muscle strength, lean mass, and endurance, with modest hypertrophy benefits in young adults and significant gains in older adults. It enhances performance in anaerobic tasks like the Wingate test but does not improve aerobic performance. [B | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1731306] [B | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.7759/cureus.103091] [C | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.7759/cureus.103937] [C | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.3389/fphys.2024.1496544]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Dosing regimens include 3–5 g/day for maintenance or 20 g/day for 5 days as a loading phase, followed by 3–5 g/day. These protocols are used in randomized trials to saturate muscle creatine stores and improve training outcomes. [B | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.7759/cureus.103091] [C | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.7759/cureus.103937] [A | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1640858]
- **Mechanism:** Creatine increases intramuscular phosphocreatine, enhancing energy supply during high-intensity exercise, reducing fatigue, and supporting muscle function and performance. [B | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1731306] [B | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.7759/cureus.103091] [C | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.3389/fphys.2024.1496544]
- **Safety/interactions:** No adverse effects were reported in studies using 20 g/day for 5 days in endurance athletes or triathletes. The FDA and EFSA have recognized creatine monohydrate as safe for human consumption. [A | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.3389/fimmu.2026.1743603] [C | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.3389/fphys.2024.1496544]
- **Applicability:** Evidence supports creatine use in general populations, including older adults, young adults, and those with chronic fatigue. It is effective for strength, muscle retention, and performance during resistance training. [B | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1731306] [B | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.7759/cureus.103091] [C | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.7759/cureus.103937] [A | 07_supplements/creatine | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1640858]
- **Convergence:** Not established in retrieved passages.
- **Bottom line:** KEEP / REVIEW AGAINST EVIDENCE. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-064"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.15 · PAGE 064 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Protein / whey?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 4 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Whey protein supplementation significantly enhances muscle protein synthesis (MPS) in older men and women, particularly when leucine is enriched, supporting muscle retention during resistance training. [C | 07_supplements/eaa_bcaa | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1709867] [C | 01_food_inflammation/milk_body_composition | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1709867]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Combining muscle-targeted oral nutritional supplementation (MT-ONS) with structured exercise improves muscle strength and body composition, with protein supplementation playing a supportive role in recovery and adaptation. [B | 07_supplements/general_ods_position_stands | 10.1186/s12937-025-01244-z] [B | 01_food_inflammation/milk_body_composition | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1755943]
- **Mechanism:** Leucine-enriched protein intake activates p70S6K1 phosphorylation, increasing MPS and anabolic response to resistance training in both younger and older males. [C | 07_supplements/eaa_bcaa | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1709867] [C | 01_food_inflammation/milk_body_composition | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1709867]
- **Safety/interactions:** Protein supplements are frequently adulterated with banned substances like anabolic steroids or stimulants, posing health risks and requiring third-party testing for safety. [A | 07_supplements/supplement_contamination_testing | 10.3389/fspor.2026.1740663]
- **Applicability:** Whey protein is applicable for fat loss and muscle retention in adults, particularly when combined with resistance training and dietary strategies that support lean mass preservation. [B | 01_food_inflammation/milk_body_composition | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1755943] [C | 01_food_inflammation/milk_body_composition | 10.1007/s13679-025-00636-w]
- **Convergence:** Not established in retrieved passages.
- **Bottom line:** FOOD VEHICLE / OPTIONAL. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-065"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.16 · PAGE 065 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Tirzepatide (current prescription)?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 3 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Tirzepatide at 10 mg and 15 mg doses resulted in mean weight reductions of 22.2 kg (21.4% TWL) and 23.6 kg (22.5% TWL), respectively, in adults without diabetes over 72 weeks; placebo group showed only 2.4 kg reduction. [C | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.1007/s40265-024-02029-0] [A | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.1503/cmaj.250502]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Tirzepatide is indicated for adults with overweight or obesity (BMI ≥30, or ≥27 with comorbidities) who have difficulty maintaining weight loss through behavioral intervention alone. [A | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.7759/cureus.109350] [C | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.1007/s40265-024-02029-0]
- **Mechanism:** Tirzepatide is a dual agonist of GLP-1R and GIP-R, reducing appetite, energy intake, and improving glucose metabolism, leading to clinical weight loss. [A | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.7759/cureus.109350] [C | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.1007/s40265-024-02029-0]
- **Safety/interactions:** Common adverse events include mild-to-moderate nausea, diarrhea, and constipation, mostly during dose escalation; discontinuation rates were 4.3% (5 mg), 7.1% (10 mg), and 6.2% (15 mg); rare cholecystitis occurred in 0.6% of users. [C | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.1007/s13300-026-01865-5] [A | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.3389/fphar.2026.1735987] [A | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.1503/cmaj.250502]
- **Applicability:** Tirzepatide is suitable for individuals with overweight or obesity and weight-related comorbidities, including those aiming for fat loss while preserving muscle, based on evidence in general adult populations. [A | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.7759/cureus.109350] [C | 05_fat_loss_drugs/tirzepatide_incretins | 10.1007/s40265-024-02029-0]
- **Convergence:** Not established in retrieved passages.
- **Bottom line:** KEEP-PRESCRIPTION — PRESCRIBER MANAGED; DO NOT CHANGE DOSE. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-066"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.17 · PAGE 066 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Vitamin D3?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 5 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Vitamin D3 supplementation shows limited ergogenic benefits in athletes, with significant improvements only in lower-limb strength among those deficient at baseline; no consistent effect on muscle hypertrophy or strength gains beyond resistance training alone. [A | 12_population_AA | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1675654] [A | 12_population_AA | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1808314]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Supplementation with 50,000 IU fortnightly (3,570 IU/day) increased serum 25(OH)D but only improved weighted reverse-grip chin-up performance; trials in older adults and burn patients show improved muscle strength and physical performance with supplementation, particularly in those with low baseline levels. [A | 12_population_AA | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1675654] [A | 12_population_AA | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1808314]
- **Mechanism:** Vitamin D enhances mitochondrial function in muscle cells by increasing oxygen consumption and ATP generation; it may also improve muscle strength via VDR-mediated regulation of protein synthesis and modulation of PTH, which influences muscle fiber atrophy and inflammation. [C | 07_supplements/vitamin_d3 | 10.1007/s00421-019-04104-x] [C | 07_supplements/vitamin_d3 | 10.3390/nu2070693]
- **Safety/interactions:** No significant adverse events related to vitamin D deficiency were reported in tirzepatide trials; no TEAEs for vitamin D deficiency were noted in 3141 participants, though baseline deficiency status was not confirmed. [B | 12_population_AA | 10.1016/j.obpill.2026.100248] [B | 12_population_AA | 10.1371/journal.pone.0333343]
- **Applicability:** Vitamin D3 supplementation may benefit individuals with baseline deficiency, particularly in improving muscle strength and function; however, no benefit was observed in those with adequate baseline levels, suggesting context-specific utility. [C | 07_supplements/vitamin_d3 | 10.3390/nu2070693] [A | 12_population_AA | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1675654]
- **Convergence:** Two independent RCTs in athletes and older adults report that vitamin D3 supplementation improves muscle strength only in those with low baseline levels, indicating convergent evidence for a deficiency-dependent effect. [A | 12_population_AA | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1675654] [A | 12_population_AA | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1808314]
- **Predeclared gate:** Test 25(OH)D or establish clinician-documented deficiency; do not assume from skin tone or indoor work.
- **Bottom line:** TEST / INTAKE / CLINICIAN GATE. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-067"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.18 · PAGE 067 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Omega-3 EPA and DHA?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 2 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Omega-3 EPA and DHA supplementation at doses of 1.5–2.0 g EPA/day may improve depression remission rates and reduce cardiovascular events, particularly in subgroups with low baseline levels; however, no significant effect on cognitive performance in Alzheimer’s disease was observed in meta-analyses. [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.1007/s11883-025-01363-2] [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1581848] [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.3390/nu17223506]
- **Retrieved studies used:** EPA-only formulations show stronger dose–response benefits for weight, BMI, and waist circumference than EPA:DHA combinations; EPA alone significantly reduces CVD risk in diabetes, while EPA+DHA shows variable effects across trials. [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1581848] [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.1007/s11883-025-01363-2] [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1748196]
- **Mechanism:** EPA and DHA modulate lipid metabolism by suppressing SREBP1c, reduce inflammation, lower triglycerides, and improve cerebrovascular health; EPA may have superior anti-inflammatory and metabolic effects compared to DHA. [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.1038/s41598-025-23677-6]
- **Safety/interactions:** Doses above 1 g/day increase risk of atrial fibrillation; common side effects include gastrointestinal discomfort, belching, and nausea; EFSA confirms up to 5,000 mg/day is safe, but doses below 2 capsules (1 g) per day show no benefit. [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.1038/s41598-025-23677-6] [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.3390/nu17223506]
- **Applicability:** Benefit is greater in individuals with low dietary intake, low blood levels, or comorbidities like depression or diabetes; subgroups with low baseline PUFA levels or inattentive symptoms may particularly benefit. [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.1007/s11883-025-01363-2] [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1581848] [C | 01_food_inflammation/omega3 | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1748196]
- **Convergence:** Not established in retrieved passages.
- **Bottom line:** OPTIONAL — NO CURRENT NEED. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-068"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.19 · PAGE 068 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Magnesium?

- **Coverage:** WEAK; best retrieved grade B; 1 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Magnesium supplementation may improve glycemic control and reduce depression scores in deficient individuals, but evidence for direct impact on fat loss, muscle retention, or running performance is not established. [B | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1765308] [C | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.1007/s13668-026-00745-5]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Oral magnesium supplementation is used in pre-diabetes, hypomagnesemia, and alcohol-related liver disease, with RCTs showing benefits in serum markers and mood, but not hard clinical endpoints. [B | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1765308] [C | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1709477] [C | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.1007/s12011-025-04678-y]
- **Mechanism:** Magnesium supports mitochondrial function, reduces oxidative stress, enhances neuromuscular performance via muscle bioavailability, and modulates HPA axis activity to reduce cortisol and anxiety. [C | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.1007/s13668-026-00745-5] [C | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.1007/s12011-025-04678-y] [C | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.3389/fendo.2024.1406455]
- **Safety/interactions:** Gastrointestinal adverse events (e.g., diarrhea) are common with magnesium oxide; hypermagnesemia risk exists in renal impairment, requiring dose titration. [B | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1765308] [C | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1709477] [C | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.20945/2359-4292-2025-0374]
- **Applicability:** Evidence supports use in hypomagnesemia, pre-diabetes, and stress-related disorders, but not in healthy individuals without deficiency or in populations with normal baseline magnesium. [B | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1765308] [C | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.1007/s13668-026-00745-5] [C | 07_supplements/magnesium | 10.1007/s12011-025-04678-y]
- **Convergence:** Not established in retrieved passages.
- **Predeclared gate:** Intake/deficiency and kidney-function gate; not an automatic sleep supplement.
- **Bottom line:** TEST / INTAKE / CLINICIAN GATE. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-069"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.20 · PAGE 069 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Citrulline / citrulline malate?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 6 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Acute citrulline malate (CM) supplementation may delay fatigue and increase repetitions to failure in high-intensity strength training, but chronic supplementation (8 weeks) shows no significant improvement in maximal strength or muscle contractile properties in healthy individuals. Mixed findings across studies suggest limited consistent performance enhancement. [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3390/nu16193235] [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.1080/15502783.2025.2513944]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Citrulline malate is commonly used in pre-workout supplements at doses ranging from 2–8 g, typically ingested 60 minutes before exercise to optimize plasma levels. Commercial products vary in citrulline-to-malate ratios, potentially affecting outcomes. [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3390/nu16193235] [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.1038/s41598-025-16374-x] [C | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1788549]
- **Mechanism:** Citrulline enhances nitric oxide (NO) production via the citrulline-arginine cycle, improving blood flow and oxygen delivery. Malate reduces lactic acid accumulation, supporting ATP generation and delaying fatigue during high-intensity exercise. [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3390/nu16193235] [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.1038/s41598-025-16374-x]
- **Safety/interactions:** No adverse events were reported in studies involving citrulline supplementation in general or diabetic populations. No significant liver enzyme changes or toxicity were observed in trials up to 8 weeks. [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3390/nu16193235] [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.1080/15502783.2025.2513944] [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3390/nu17172790]
- **Applicability:** Citrulline supplementation shows benefit in middle-aged and older adults with type 2 diabetes, improving microvascular function and muscle strength. Evidence in healthy, younger populations like Michael (26) remains limited and not directly established. [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3390/nu17172790] [A | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1632952]
- **Convergence:** Two independent human studies (S6, S8) indicate citrulline improves microvascular function and endothelial health in older adults with T2D, suggesting a consistent direction for vascular benefits in this subgroup. [B | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3390/nu17172790] [A | 07_supplements/citrulline_arginine | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1632952]
- **Bottom line:** SHORTLIST FOR REVIEW. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-070"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.21 · PAGE 070 — What does the retrieved evidence show for HMB?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 6 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** HMB supplementation in surgical patients was associated with reduced length of hospital stay and improved muscle strength, but not consistently for physical function or mortality; benefits were observed in elderly or frail populations but not in critically ill patients. [A | 07_supplements/hmb | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1621206]
- **Retrieved studies used:** HMB was studied in surgical and elderly/frail populations, with meta-analyses showing benefit in muscle mass and function, but not in critically ill or ICU patients, where no mortality or functional improvement was observed. [A | 07_supplements/hmb | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1621206]
- **Mechanism:** HMB stimulates mTOR-mediated protein synthesis, inhibits proteolysis via ubiquitin–proteasome pathway, supports mitochondrial biogenesis, and may enhance fatty acid oxidation and aerobic metabolism. [C | 07_supplements/hmb | 10.1007/s12013-026-01995-4] [A | 07_supplements/hmb | 10.1186/s12891-025-09436-8]
- **Safety/interactions:** Daily HMB intake up to 3 g is considered safe and well-tolerated; no major adverse effects reported in trials, though long-term effects and optimal duration remain unclear. [A | 07_supplements/hmb | 10.1186/s12891-025-09436-8]
- **Applicability:** HMB may benefit surgical and elderly populations with muscle loss, but not critically ill patients; evidence for performance enhancement in healthy, active individuals is indirect and not established. [A | 07_supplements/hmb | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1621206]
- **Convergence:** Not established in retrieved passages.
- **Bottom line:** SHORTLIST FOR REVIEW. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-071"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.22 · PAGE 071 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Beta-alanine?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 6 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Beta-alanine supplementation shows trivial or no significant effect on mean repeated sprint performance (SMD −0.018), peak sprint power (SMD 0.205), or fatigue index (SMD −0.020), with low to very low certainty evidence across 10–17 RCTs. No consistent improvement in sprint time or jump performance was observed in general populations. [A | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1818755] [A | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1636970]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Beta-alanine was used in 3–10-week RCTs at 3.2–6.4 g/day, primarily in trained athletes (e.g., futsal, kickboxing, cyclists), with most studies using 4–6-week loading phases and placebo-controlled designs. [A | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1818755] [B | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.1371/journal.pone.0346898]
- **Mechanism:** Beta-alanine increases muscle carnosine, which buffers intramuscular H+ during high-intensity exercise, potentially improving performance in repeated explosive efforts. It may also support β-cell function and glucose metabolism, though effects on insulin resistance remain limited. [A | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1818755] [A | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.1186/s12902-025-02016-w] [B | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.1080/15502783.2025.2564245]
- **Safety/interactions:** No adverse events were reported in RCTs using 3.2–6.4 g/day for 3–10 weeks. Paresthesia was noted in some studies, mitigated by sustained-release formulations. No serious safety concerns were observed in general populations. [A | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1818755] [B | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.1371/journal.pone.0346898]
- **Applicability:** Beta-alanine may benefit athletes in sports requiring repeated high-intensity efforts (e.g., kickboxing, futsal, cycling), but not consistently in sprint performance or jump outcomes. Not established for fat loss, appetite, or blood sugar control in non-diabetic individuals. [A | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1818755] [A | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1636970]
- **Convergence:** Both S3 and S4 report trivial or no effect of beta-alanine on repeated sprint performance and jump outcomes, with S3 showing low-certainty evidence and S4 showing moderate-certainty evidence, converging on limited ergogenic benefit in general populations. [A | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1818755] [A | 07_supplements/beta_alanine | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1636970]
- **Bottom line:** SHORTLIST FOR REVIEW. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-072"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.23 · PAGE 072 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Alpha-GPC?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 5 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Alpha-GPC supplementation for 6 days increased lower body isometric force production in healthy young males; a trend toward improved upper body strength was observed, but findings require replication with larger samples and power-based metrics. [B | 07_supplements/alpha_gpc | 10.1186/s12970-015-0103-x]
- **Retrieved studies used:** Six-day supplementation with alpha-GPC was studied in a double-blind, placebo-controlled crossover trial involving 13 healthy college-aged males; the intervention focused on isometric strength performance. [B | 07_supplements/alpha_gpc | 10.1186/s12970-015-0103-x]
- **Mechanism:** Alpha-GPC may support cognitive function via acetylcholine synthesis and neuroprotection; alterations in phosphatidylserine and alpha-GPC levels suggest roles in membrane remodeling and choline metabolism, potentially under hyperoxic stress. [C | 07_supplements/alpha_gpc | 10.3389/fmicb.2025.1479992] [B | 07_supplements/alpha_gpc | 10.1038/s41598-026-44520-6]
- **Safety/interactions:** No adverse events were reported in a 1-week daily intake of an energy drink containing alpha-GPC; however, no direct safety data on alpha-GPC alone were provided in the retrieved passages. [A | 07_supplements/alpha_gpc | 10.1080/15502783.2023.2171314] [B | 07_supplements/alpha_gpc | 10.1038/s41598-026-38979-6]
- **Applicability:** Alpha-GPC has not been studied in populations with fat loss, running performance, or strength retention during cutting; evidence is limited to general healthy adults and not specific to endurance, strength, or metabolic goals. [C | 07_supplements/alpha_gpc | 10.3389/fnut.2026.1741204] [A | 07_supplements/alpha_gpc | 10.1080/15502783.2023.2171314]
- **Convergence:** Not established in retrieved passages.
- **Bottom line:** OPTIONAL — NOT SHORTLISTED. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.

<a id="hc-page-073"></a>
### CHAPTER IV.24 · PAGE 073 — What does the retrieved evidence show for Ashwagandha (KSM-66 or Sensoril)?

- **Coverage:** STRONG; best retrieved grade A; 4 unique A/B human source(s); population fit direct/general.
- **Outcomes and magnitude:** Ashwagandha (KSM-66 or Sensoril) supplementation showed significant improvements in muscle strength, VO2 max, time to exhaustion, and physical performance metrics in resistance-trained individuals, sprinters, and cyclists, with consistent positive trends across multiple trials. [C | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.3389/fnut.2024.1439294] [C | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.1186/s12986-025-00902-7]
- **Retrieved studies used:** The studies used 300 mg twice daily of KSM-66 Ashwagandha root extract in capsule form, administered over 8–12 weeks to healthy adults, including resistance-trained males, sprinters, and elite cyclists. [B | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.3389/frph.2025.1647721] [C | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.3389/fnut.2024.1439294]
- **Mechanism:** Ashwagandha reduces cortisol levels, improves stress and sleep, exhibits anti-inflammatory and antioxidant effects via NF-κB inhibition and increased SOD, and may enhance muscle cell differentiation and hormonal balance. [C | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.1186/s12986-025-00902-7] [C | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.1007/s10522-025-10320-0]
- **Safety/interactions:** No significant adverse events were reported in trials up to 60 days; hematological and serological profiles remained normal; AI-driven meta-analysis confirms safe use with no carcinogenic, teratogenic, or mutagenic effects. [B | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.3389/frph.2025.1647721] [B | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.7759/cureus.68940] [A | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.3389/fnut.2025.1658265]
- **Applicability:** The evidence supports use in healthy adults, including athletes and those in resistance training, for improving strength, endurance, and performance, with relevance to Michael’s goals of muscle retention and running performance. [B | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.3389/frph.2025.1647721] [C | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.3389/fnut.2024.1439294] [C | 07_supplements/ashwagandha | 10.1186/s12986-025-00902-7]
- **Convergence:** Not established in retrieved passages.
- **Bottom line:** OPTIONAL — NOT SHORTLISTED. This is an evidence-screening decision, not a prescription.
